**Definition (learn):** The question asks for comment with illustrations on how policy contradictions among competing sectors and stakeholders lead to inadequate environmental protection and prevention of degradation.
**Breakdown:**
- Mechanism: sectoral ministries optimise their own objectives; no single actor is accountable for the aggregate outcome; environmental cost is an externality absent from departmental metrics.
- Illustrations: input subsidy for water-intensive crops vs groundwater conservation; energy access vs emission commitments; clearance timelines vs impact assessment depth.
- Correctives: RIA; inter-ministerial appraisal; district convergence; outcome indicators with environmental externality.
- Counterpoint: some contradictions are value conflicts, not coordination failures.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 10 marks, 150 words; verified transcription from books/more_previous_papers/GENERAL-STUDIES-PAPER-II.pdf, PDF p. 2 (✅ owner 02).
**Links:** GOV-1058 (RIA), GOV-1049 (convergence), GOV-1073 (value conflicts).
**Trap:** Do not invent scheme names or degradation statistics. Environmental law (EIA/NGT) belongs to Environment and Ecology.
**Mains hook:** Distinguish a coordination failure from a value conflict; that distinction lifts the answer.
**Back:** *"Contradictory policies, invisible externality: coordination or value?"*

### Flashcard GOV-1073 — Value conflict vs coordination failure
**Front:** When is a policy contradiction a value conflict rather than a coordination failure?
**Definition (learn):** Some contradictions are genuine value conflicts, such as livelihood today against ecological stock tomorrow. These cannot be resolved by better process alone.
**Breakdown:**
- Coordination failure: fixable through process, RIA and convergence.
- Value conflict: requires a political choice of priority.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 02 analytical distinction (⚠️).
**Links:** GOV-1072, GOV-1049, GOV-1058.
**Trap:** Presenting a value conflict as merely a process problem.
**Mains hook:** State which kind the contradiction is before recommending a corrective.
**Back:** *"Process fixes coordination; value conflicts need a choice."*

### Flashcard GOV-1074 — 2022 GS-II Q6: Gati Shakti and government-private coordination
**Front:** What does the 2022 GS-II Q6 ask, and how does the answer classify the problem?
**Definition (learn):** The question asks for a discussion of meticulous coordination between government and the private sector for connectivity under Gati Shakti.
**Breakdown:**
- Classification: coordination and information-asymmetry problem, not infrastructure finance.
- PM GatiShakti: National Master Plan for multi-modal connectivity; shared GIS-based planning platform.
- Mechanism: removes sequencing failure ("dig once") and reduces private-sector information risk.
- Limitation: coordinates information, not authority; land and approvals stay with States.
- PIB Lok Sabha reply 11 August 2026 (PRID 2297818): integrated planning framework, no separate budgetary provision, Network Planning Group evaluates critical projects.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Verified transcription, 10 marks (✅ owner 02; books/more_previous_papers/QP-CSM-22-GENERAL-STUDIES-PAPER-II-190922.pdf, PDF p. 2). PIB reply (✅ owner 02; not re-fetched).
**Links:** GOV-1049 (convergence), GOV-1075 (PPP governance block), GOV-1076 (platform coordinates information, not authority).
**Trap:** A planning platform is not a source of funding; do not carry undated investment or project figures.
**Mains hook:** The state's role is de-risking through predictability, not subsidy.
**Back:** *"One map, many ministries, no extra authority."*

### Flashcard GOV-1075 — PPP governance block: from output standard to exit rules
**Front:** What is the PPP governance sequence the owner recommends?
**Definition (learn):** A governance chain for PPPs: public purpose and output standard, risk allocation, competitive procurement with disclosed criteria, performance-linked payment with measurable service standard, independent monitoring and contract disclosure, renegotiation/dispute/exit rules, and affordability, access and continuity safeguards.
**Breakdown:**
- Why PPP: public authority plus private capital, technology or operating capability.
- Core risks: optimistic demand forecasts; contingent liabilities; weak competition; opportunistic renegotiation; information asymmetry; user-fee exclusion; accountability fragmentation.
- Viability Gap Funding: supports economically justified but commercially weak projects.
- Hybrid Annuity Model: construction/operation risk largely with concessionaire; government carries revenue payment.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 02 (✅ owner 02). No project success rate asserted.
**Links:** GOV-1074, GOV-1076, GOV-1077 (PPP is procurement).
**Trap:** PPP is not one uniform risk transfer and not privatisation by definition.
**Mains hook:** Defend PPP only where outputs, risk, monitoring, affordability and exit are specified before award.
**Back:** *"Output, risk, bid, pay for performance, watch, exit, afford."*

### Flashcard GOV-1076 — Trap: a planning platform coordinates information, not authority
**Front:** Can a shared planning platform resolve federal or jurisdictional friction?
**Definition (learn):** No. A planning platform coordinates information and visibility. Land, utilities and many approvals remain with States and local bodies.
**Breakdown:**
- Improves: visibility, sequencing and predictability.
- Does not: transfer authority, resolve federal friction or prove outcomes.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 02 limitation (⚠️ analytical).
**Links:** GOV-1074, GOV-1075.
**Trap:** Visibility is not authority; visibility is not completion or lower logistics cost.
**Mains hook:** Name the authority still missing when a coordination tool is proposed.
**Back:** *"A map shows the road; it doesn't own the land."*

### Flashcard GOV-1077 — PPP is a procurement and service-delivery choice, not privatisation by definition
**Front:** Is PPP the same as privatisation?
**Definition (learn):** No. PPP is a procurement and service-delivery choice. It is defensible only where output, risk, monitoring, affordability and exit are specified before award.
**Breakdown:**
- Public purpose and output standard remain public.
- Risk is allocated to the party best able to manage it.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 02 judgement (⚠️).
**Links:** GOV-1075, GOV-1074.
**Trap:** Equating PPP with asset sale.
**Mains hook:** Specify output, risk, monitoring and exit before arguing for or against PPP.
**Back:** *"PPP is a contract for outputs, not a sale of the public purpose."*

### Flashcard GOV-1078 — Universalism vs targeting: the identification-cost trade-off
**Front:** What is the trade-off between universal and targeted entitlements?
**Definition (learn):** Targeting concentrates scarce resources but needs identification machinery that can itself exclude. Universalism avoids identification error but costs more and can be regressive in incidence.
**Breakdown:**
- Resolution: choose by the ratio of identification cost to benefit value.
- Universalise where identification cost approaches the benefit.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 02 trade-off bank (⚠️ analytical).
**Links:** GOV-1050, GOV-1063, GOV-1079 (speed vs consultation).
**Trap:** Targeting is not automatically efficient; it can exclude.
**Mains hook:** State the identification cost before choosing.
**Back:** *"Target if identifying is cheap; universalise if it costs the benefit."*

### Flashcard GOV-1079 — Speed vs consultation, discretion vs uniformity, centre vs local trade-offs
**Front:** What three trade-offs does the owner list alongside universalism vs targeting?
**Definition (learn):** Speed vs consultation; discretion vs uniformity; centralised design vs local adaptation.
**Breakdown:**
- Speed vs consultation: time-bound consultation with a published response matrix.
- Discretion vs uniformity: guided discretion with written reasons, recorded decisions and appeal.
- Centre vs local: decide entitlement centrally; decide delivery method locally.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 02 trade-off bank (⚠️ analytical).
**Links:** GOV-1056, GOV-1043, GOV-1044.
**Trap:** Treating any trade-off as a binary choice.
**Mains hook:** Name the trade-off and the governance resolution, not just the tension.
**Back:** *"Fast or consulted, free or guided, central or local: the middle path."*

### Flashcard GOV-1080 — Pilot-to-scale test
**Front:** What must a pilot test before a state-wide or national scale-up?
**Definition (learn):** A pilot tests a design under bounded conditions. Scaling asks whether the result survives ordinary budgets, staffing, federal variation, procurement, citizen diversity and weaker supervision.
**Breakdown:**
- Pre-specify: output, outcome, equity, cost and failure indicators.
- Compare under ordinary, not exceptional, staffing.
- Expand in phases across different State and local contexts.
- Preserve a stop, redesign or sunset decision.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 02 (⚠️ analytical).
**Links:** GOV-1046, GOV-1041, GOV-1081 (pilot fidelity vs scalability).
**Trap:** Replication of activity is not replication of causal effect. Pilot success is evidence for adaptation, not proof of scalability.
**Mains hook:** A successful district pilot does not guarantee a national result.
**Back:** *"Pilot proves the idea, scale proves the system."*

### Flashcard GOV-1081 — Trap: pilot success equals scalability
**Front:** Does a successful pilot prove a national programme will work?
**Definition (learn):** No. Exceptional staff, close supervision, narrow beneficiary selection and short horizons can inflate pilot success.
**Breakdown:**
- Inflators: selection effects, exceptional staff, short time horizon, close monitoring.
- Scale tests: heterogeneous sites, full fiscal cost, equity, grievance load.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 02 advanced (⚠️).
**Links:** GOV-1080, GOV-1041.
**Trap:** Mistaking a well-supervised pilot for a national mechanism.
**Mains hook:** Demand heterogeneous-site evidence before scaling.
**Back:** *"Pilot's best staff are not the national staff."*

### Flashcard GOV-1082 — Trap: more clearance points mean more accountability
**Front:** Does adding clearance points improve accountability?
**Definition (learn):** No. Additional clearance points multiply failure probability without necessarily adding proportional accountability value.
**Breakdown:**
- Effect: delay and distortion risk rises.
- Accountability needs identifiable duty-holders and remedies, not just approvals.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 02 advanced trap (✅ owner 02).
**Links:** GOV-1044, GOV-1023 (duty-holder at stage 2).
**Trap:** Confusing procedural layers with accountability.
**Mains hook:** Accountability is clarity of duty and remedy, not the count of approvals.
**Back:** *"More signatures, more failure, not more accountability."*

### Flashcard GOV-1083 — Trap: street-level discretion is always a failure to be eliminated
**Front:** Should governance aim to eliminate street-level discretion?
**Definition (learn):** No. Lipsky treats discretion as unavoidable and potentially adaptive. The governance task is to guide it through rules, written reasons, records and appeal.
**Breakdown:**
- Guidance tools: clear entitlement rules, reasoned rejection, recorded decisions, appeal.
- Effect: humane adaptation, not arbitrary rationing.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 02 (✅ owner 02, Lipsky).
**Links:** GOV-1043, GOV-1047, GOV-1079.
**Trap:** Eliminating discretion entirely removes adaptive capacity.
**Mains hook:** Argue for guided discretion rather than rule-only design.
**Back:** *"Guide the discretion; don't abolish the clerk."*

### Flashcard GOV-1084 — Concept card: policy design and implementation in one line
**Front:** In one sentence, what is the Governance file 02 concept?
**Definition (learn):** The implementation gap is a predictable product of design realism, clearance-point count and street-level discretion quality, not merely of intent or corruption.
**Breakdown:**
- Design realism.
- Clearance points.
- Street-level discretion.
- Evaluation feedback closes the loop.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 02 thesis (✅ owner 02).
**Links:** GOV-1041, GOV-1042, GOV-1044, GOV-1043.
**Trap:** Treating implementation as a post-design afterthought.
**Mains hook:** Open with the thesis; then locate the stage.
**Back:** *"Design realism, clearance points, discretion: the three levers of the gap."*

### GROUP — Development Processes and the Development Industry

### Flashcard GOV-1085 — Development industry: the non-state delivery ecosystem
**Front:** What is the "development industry", and who belongs to it?
**Definition (learn):** The ecosystem of donors, multilateral and bilateral agencies, charitable trusts, foundations, corporate CSR programmes and implementing NGOs that fund and deliver development alongside the state.
**Breakdown:**
- Bilateral and multilateral donors (World Bank, ADB, UN agencies, bilateral aid).
- Public charitable trusts and endowments (state public-trust law).
- Private foundations and family philanthropy.
- Corporate social responsibility (Companies Act 2013, s.135).
- Delivery through NGOs and SHGs (see GOV-1120 onward in file 04).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Ecosystem definition (✅ owner 03). Not a single statutory sector (✅ owner 03 semantic-completeness note).
**Links:** GOV-1086 (legal vehicles), GOV-1096 (funding-to-inclusion chain), GOV-1102 (development finance institutions).
**Trap:** "Development industry" is an analytical label, not a statute or a regulator.
**Mains hook:** Map funders, intermediaries and implementers separately before judging inclusion.
**Back:** *"Donors, trusts, foundations, CSR, NGOs: the state is one actor among several."*

### Flashcard GOV-1086 — Public charitable trust: deed and state law, not the Indian Trusts Act
**Front:** What governs a public charitable trust in India?
**Definition (learn):** A trust or endowment for a public charitable purpose, governed by the trust deed, the applicable state public-trust or endowment law and general legal principles.
**Breakdown:**
- Governing instruments: trust deed, state public-trust/endowment law.
- Founder-defined purpose.
- Regime varies by State.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 (✅ owner 03). Section 1 of the Indian Trusts Act, 1882 expressly excludes public or private religious or charitable endowments (✅ owner 03).
**Links:** GOV-1087 (Trusts Act trap), GOV-1088 (Societies), GOV-1089 (Section 8 company).
**Trap:** Do not cite the Indian Trusts Act, 1882 as the national incorporation statute for public charitable trusts.
**Mains hook:** Trust governance is weaker externally than company law; the regime varies by State.
**Back:** *"Deed plus state law; the 1882 Act steps aside for charity."*

### Flashcard GOV-1087 — Trap: the Indian Trusts Act, 1882 does not govern public charitable endowments
**Front:** Does the Indian Trusts Act, 1882 apply to public charitable or religious endowments?
**Definition (learn):** No. Section 1 excludes public or private religious or charitable endowments from the Act's scope.
**Breakdown:**
- The Act principally concerns private trusts.
- Charitable and religious endowments are governed by state law and deed.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Section 1, Indian Trusts Act 1882 (✅ owner 03).
**Links:** GOV-1086.
**Trap:** A common prelims error is to name the 1882 Act as the statute for charitable trusts.
**Mains hook:** Cite state public-trust law and the deed for charitable endowments.
**Back:** *"Section 1 excludes charity; cite state law."*

### Flashcard GOV-1088 — Society: Societies Registration Act, 1860 and state variants
**Front:** What is a society, and under which law is it registered?
**Definition (learn):** A voluntary association of persons, registered for a charitable, literary, scientific or similar object, under the Societies Registration Act, 1860 or state variants. It is a common legal form for NGOs.
**Breakdown:**
- Governance: membership and general body.
- Accountability route: general-body and registrar.
- Common NGO form in India.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Societies Registration Act, 1860 (✅ owner 03).
**Links:** GOV-1086, GOV-1089, GOV-1093 (legal forms trap), GOV-1119 (comparison).
**Trap:** Registration as a society is not tax exemption and not foreign-funding permission (owner 03 evidence bank).
**Mains hook:** Society governance is membership-based; compare its accountability with a Section 8 company's board governance.
**Back:** *"Eighteen sixty, members govern, registration is not permission."*

### Flashcard GOV-1089 — Section 8 company: not-for-profit under the Companies Act, 2013
**Front:** What is a Section 8 company, and what is its key restriction?
**Definition (learn):** A company registered under Section 8 of the Companies Act, 2013 to promote charitable objects. It is prohibited from distributing profits to its members.
**Breakdown:**
- Governance: board governance under the Companies Act.
- Disclosure: Companies Act disclosure norms.
- Users: larger, professionally governed NGOs and foundations.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Section 8, Companies Act 2013 (✅ owner 03).
**Links:** GOV-1086, GOV-1088, GOV-1090 (CSR), GOV-1116 (CSR-2 reporting).
**Trap:** A Section 8 company is not an ordinary for-profit company; it cannot pay dividends to members.
**Mains hook:** Section 8 governance is more disclosed than a trust; compliance costs are higher.
**Back:** *"Section eight: charity by company, no dividends."*

### Flashcard GOV-1090 — CSR under section 135: at least 2% of average net profits
**Front:** What is the statutory CSR minimum under section 135 of the Companies Act, 2013?
**Definition (learn):** Companies meeting specified net worth, turnover or net profit thresholds must spend at least 2% of their average net profits of the preceding years on CSR activities.
**Breakdown:**
- Basis: average net profits of preceding financial years.
- Minimum rate: 2%.
- Applicability: specified net worth, turnover or net profit thresholds.
- Activities: Schedule VII list (GOV-1091).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2% of average net profits (✅ owner 03; Companies Act 2013 s.135). Thresholds are set by CSR rules, not quoted here (⚠️ not re-fetched).
**Links:** GOV-1091, GOV-1092, GOV-1100 (visible-need bias), GOV-1116.
**Trap:** The 2% is a minimum spend, not an upper cap; quote the threshold from the current rules, not from s.135 alone.
**Mains hook:** CSR is a statutory floor, which means CSR funding is a state-mandated private channel, not charity alone.
**Back:** *"Section 135: two per cent of average profits, if you cross the bar."*

### Flashcard GOV-1091 — Schedule VII of the Companies Act, 2013: permitted CSR activities
**Front:** What does Schedule VII of the Companies Act, 2013 do in the CSR framework?
**Definition (learn):** It lists the permitted CSR activity areas that companies may spend on.
**Breakdown:**
- Examples named by owner 03: education, health, poverty, environment, rural development, disaster relief, and more.
- Effect: prevents diversion of CSR funds to non-developmental corporate interest.
- Side effect: crowds funders into popular areas (GOV-1100).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Schedule VII (✅ owner 03). Full list not reproduced here (⚠️).
**Links:** GOV-1090, GOV-1092, GOV-1100.
**Trap:** Any company-chosen initiative is not CSR; CSR spend must fall within Schedule VII.
**Mains hook:** The activity list bounds CSR, both protecting against diversion and narrowing the field.
**Back:** *"Schedule VII: the menu; anything off-menu is not CSR."*

### Flashcard GOV-1092 — Trap: CSR is a statutory obligation, not voluntary philanthropy
**Front:** Is CSR voluntary corporate charity with no legal basis?
**Definition (learn):** No. Since the Companies Act 2013, CSR spending is a statutory obligation for companies crossing the thresholds (s.135).
**Breakdown:**
- Statutory: minimum spend, Board disclosure, CSR-2 reporting.
- Voluntary philanthropy: trusts, foundations, individual giving; no statutory floor or activity restriction.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** s.135 (✅ owner 03).
**Links:** GOV-1090, GOV-1091, GOV-1116.
**Trap:** Describing CSR as optional charity loses the legal distinction.
**Mains hook:** Contrast statutory CSR with voluntary philanthropy to show where accountability differs.
**Back:** *"CSR is an obligation; philanthropy is a choice."*

### Flashcard GOV-1093 — Trap: trust, society, Section 8 company and NGO are not legally identical
**Front:** Are NGOs, societies, trusts and Section 8 companies the same?
**Definition (learn):** No. They are distinct legal forms under different regimes: state trust or endowment law, the Societies Registration Act, and the Companies Act 2013.
**Breakdown:**
- Trust: deed and state law.
- Society: 1860 Act and state variants.
- Section 8 company: Companies Act 2013.
- NGO is a generic label, not a legal form.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 (✅).
**Links:** GOV-1086, GOV-1088, GOV-1089, GOV-1119.
**Trap:** Using "NGO" as if it were a statutory form.
**Mains hook:** Always name the legal form before assessing accountability.
**Back:** *"NGO is a label; the law sorts the forms."*

### Flashcard GOV-1094 — Donor-driven vs demand-driven development
**Front:** What is the difference between donor-driven and demand-driven development?
**Definition (learn):** Donor-driven development follows the funding priorities of the donor or foundation. Demand-driven development responds to locally articulated need.
**Breakdown:**
- Donor-driven: designed around funder theory of change and reporting metrics.
- Demand-driven: designed around local priorities.
- Divergence risk: project-based funding cycles mismatch long-term local need.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 (⚠️ analytical distinction).
**Links:** GOV-1099 (donor distortion channels), GOV-1098 (need-fit test), GOV-1109 (PYQ 2022).
**Trap:** Donor-funded development is not the same as demand-driven development.
**Mains hook:** Use it as the critical counterpoint to any claim that the development industry improves inclusion.
**Back:** *"Funder's priority or the local need: which sets the agenda?"*

### Flashcard GOV-1095 — Substitution vs complementarity thesis
**Front:** What are the substitution and complementarity theses about non-state development?
**Definition (learn):** The substitution thesis treats charitable and CSR development as filling gaps left by weak state capacity, a symptom of state failure. The complementarity thesis treats it as a permanent and desirable division of labour, where donors bring flexibility and innovation.
**Breakdown:**
- Substitution: gap-filling, may relieve pressure to fix state delivery.
- Complementarity: flexibility, speed, innovation; efficient division of labour.
- Replies: additionality test (substitution); convergence and disclosure (complementarity).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 (⚠️ analytical, not a cited study).
**Links:** GOV-1098 (additionality), GOV-1111 (complementarity case), GOV-1113.
**Trap:** Mains answers must choose and justify a position; a neutral list loses marks.
**Mains hook:** Take a position and test it with additionality.
**Back:** *"Gap-filler or partner: choose, then test additionality."*

### Flashcard GOV-1096 — Funding to inclusion chain: five stages from funder to outcome
**Front:** Trace the funding-to-outcome causal chain set by owner 03.
**Definition (learn):** The chain is: funding source sets the incentive frame → legal vehicle sets disclosure and governance quality → implementing NGO or SHG sets last-mile fidelity → accountability route (upward, not downward) → aggregate outcome (converged and inclusive, or fragmented and duplicative).
**Breakdown:**
- Funding source: project cycle, founder priority or Schedule VII compliance.
- Vehicle: trust < society < Section 8 company in external scrutiny (owner 03 ordering).
- Implementer: local social capital and capacity (see file 04).
- Accountability: donor and regulator, not beneficiary.
- Outcome: inclusion or fragmentation.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 causal chain (⚠️ analytical).
**Links:** GOV-1085, GOV-1086, GOV-1097, GOV-1101.
**Trap:** Skipping the accountability route; funding does not automatically produce inclusion.
**Mains hook:** Trace the chain in any 15 or 20-mark answer.
**Back:** *"Funder sets incentives, vehicle sets visibility, NGO sets fidelity, upward reporting, outcome varies."*

### Flashcard GOV-1097 — Accountability substitution: upward to donors, not downward to beneficiaries
**Front:** What is accountability substitution in the development industry?
**Definition (learn):** Beneficiaries cannot vote out a trust or CSR programme. Accountability therefore runs upward to donors, trustees and regulators rather than downward to the people served.
**Breakdown:**
- No electoral sanction.
- Corrective loop: donor and regulatory oversight, at best participatory monitoring.
- Structurally weaker feedback than electoral accountability.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 thesis T2 (⚠️ analytical).
**Links:** GOV-1096, GOV-1099, GOV-1098 (accountability test).
**Trap:** Equating regulatory compliance with downward accountability.
**Mains hook:** Start any evaluation of development NGOs from the direction of accountability.
**Back:** *"Reports go up; the voice must come down."*

### Flashcard GOV-1098 — Five-test evaluation grid for non-state interventions
**Front:** What are the five tests for evaluating a non-state development intervention?
**Definition (learn):** The owner's grid: additionality, need-fit, accountability, sustainability, federal/local fit.
**Breakdown:**
- Additionality: adds capacity, or replaces a public obligation?
- Need-fit: chosen from disaggregated local exclusion data, or the funder's priority?
- Accountability: can beneficiaries get records, complain and force correction?
- Sustainability: who finances and operates after the grant cycle?
- Federal/local fit: converged with district or local plans, or a parallel silo?
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 (⚠️ analytical grid).
**Links:** GOV-1095, GOV-1097, GOV-1101, GOV-1114.
**Trap:** A funder that fails additionality and accountability is not legitimate even if it passes the other three (owner 03 novel-actor stem).
**Mains hook:** Apply the five tests verbatim to a named case.
**Back:** *"Add, need, answer, last, fit: five tests, two fatal."*

### Flashcard GOV-1099 — Four donor-distortion channels: agenda, metric, cycle, accountability substitution
**Front:** How does donor funding weaken community participation, according to owner 03?
**Definition (learn):** Four named channels: agenda channel (donor priorities set the problem before need is assessed), metric channel (countable outputs favoured, slow participation cut), cycle channel (project time-bound grants mismatch community maturation), and accountability-substitution channel (upward reporting replaces downward answerability).
**Breakdown:**
- Agenda: donor sets problem.
- Metric: donor rewards countable outputs.
- Cycle: time-bound project mismatch.
- Accountability: upward substitutes for downward.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 §13.0(b) (⚠️ analytical).
**Links:** GOV-1094, GOV-1109 (PYQ 2022), GOV-1097.
**Trap:** Donor money can also finance participation (mobilisers, SHG federations); concede this counter-evidence.
**Mains hook:** Name the channel that operates in the case; not just "donor dependence is bad".
**Back:** *"Agenda, metric, cycle, accountability: four ways donors can displace participation."*

### Flashcard GOV-1100 — Visible-need bias in CSR
**Front:** What is visible-need bias, and why does it arise?
**Definition (learn):** Schedule VII plus reputational incentives direct CSR into visible sectors (school buildings, sanitation hardware) while less legible needs (mental health, care work, legal aid, disability access) stay underfunded.
**Breakdown:**
- Cause: Schedule VII list and reputation.
- Effect: uneven coverage across needs.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 counter-argument bank (⚠️ analytical).
**Links:** GOV-1090 (s.135), GOV-1091 (Schedule VII), GOV-1098 (need-fit test).
**Trap:** A CSR-built classroom does not show that CSR serves the most needy.
**Mains hook:** Argue for need-fit and disclosure to correct visible-need bias.
**Back:** *"Classrooms get funded; mental health waits."*

### Flashcard GOV-1101 — Fragmentation: duplication without convergence
**Front:** Why can multiple donors and CSR programmes in one geography produce fragmented outcomes?
**Definition (learn):** Uncoordinated donors and CSR programmes can duplicate effort and produce visible-need bias rather than an inclusive aggregate outcome. The failure is one of convergence, not generosity.
**Breakdown:**
- Duplication of effort.
- Lack of convergence with district plans.
- Remedy: convergence with the district plan (see file 02, GOV-1049).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 thesis T4 (⚠️ analytical).
**Links:** GOV-1049, GOV-1098 (federal/local fit), GOV-1096.
**Trap:** Assuming more donors means more inclusion.
**Mains hook:** Argue convergence as the correction, not more funding.
**Back:** *"More donors, same district, duplicate effort: a convergence failure."*

### Flashcard GOV-1102 — Multilateral and bilateral development finance: World Bank, ADB, UN agencies
**Front:** Which kinds of institutions fund development in India through loans, grants and technical assistance?
**Definition (learn):** Multilateral and bilateral development finance institutions, including the World Bank, the Asian Development Bank, UNDP and bilateral aid agencies, fund government and non-government programmes.
**Breakdown:**
- Channels: project loans, grants, technical assistance.
- Recipients: government and non-government programmes.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 (⚠️ no amounts, names or conditionalities asserted).
**Links:** GOV-1085, GOV-1103 (FCRA gate), GOV-1094.
**Trap:** Do not quote project amounts or donor portfolios without a dated source.
**Mains hook:** Use these channels as a funding layer that sets an incentive frame.
**Back:** *"Loans, grants, technical help: the multilateral layer."*

### Flashcard GOV-1103 — Three regulatory layers: Darpan, FCRA and 12A/80G are different
**Front:** What do NGO Darpan, FCRA registration and Income Tax 12A/80G each do?
**Definition (learn):** Owner 03 treats these as separate layers: NGO Darpan is an executive information interface (owner 03 rechecked 5 September 2026 with MCA), FCRA governs foreign contributions (MHA), and Sections 12A and 80G govern tax exemption and donor deduction.
**Breakdown:**
- NGO Darpan: executive information interface, not a licence.
- FCRA, 2010 (amended 2020): foreign contribution gate, MHA.
- 12A/80G: tax exemption for the body and deduction for donors.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** FCRA 2010 amended 2020 (✅ owner 03). NGO Darpan and MCA sources (✅ owner 03 verified current fact).
**Links:** GOV-1104 (FCRA gate), GOV-1105 (regulatory fragmentation).
**Trap:** Darpan registration is not FCRA permission; registration is not tax exemption.
**Mains hook:** Name the layer that controls each permission before judging an NGO.
**Back:** *"Darpan informs, FCRA permits foreign money, 12A and 80G tax it."*

### Flashcard GOV-1104 — FCRA as a structural precondition for donor-funded work at scale
**Front:** Why is FCRA compliance a structural precondition for foreign-funded development work?
**Definition (learn):** The Foreign Contribution (Regulation) Act, 2010 (amended 2020), administered by the Ministry of Home Affairs, requires registration or prior permission, a designated FCRA account and limits on administrative expense and sub-granting.
**Breakdown:**
- Registration or prior permission.
- Designated FCRA account.
- Limits on administrative expense and sub-granting between FCRA entities.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** FCRA 2010, amended 2020, MHA (✅ owner 03; detail in file 04).
**Links:** GOV-1103, GOV-1113 (sovereignty objection), GOV-1106 (2024 Q6).
**Trap:** Treating FCRA as a formality; it gates foreign funding at scale. Do not assert registration counts or cancellations.
**Mains hook:** A foreign-funded trust without FCRA permission cannot receive the donor's money.
**Back:** *"No FCRA, no foreign money at scale."*

### Flashcard GOV-1105 — Regulatory fragmentation across MCA, Income Tax, state Registrars and MHA
**Front:** Which bodies oversee the development industry, and why is the fragmentation itself an analytical point?
**Definition (learn):** No single unified regulator supervises the development industry. Oversight is distributed across the Ministry of Corporate Affairs (companies, CSR), the Income Tax Department (tax-exempt entities), state Registrars (societies and state trust acts) and the Ministry of Home Affairs (foreign funding).
**Breakdown:**
- MCA: Section 8 companies and CSR compliance.
- Income Tax Department: charitable-trust tax-exemption compliance.
- State Registrars: societies and state public trusts.
- MHA: FCRA.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 advanced (✅ owner 03).
**Links:** GOV-1103, GOV-1104, GOV-1093.
**Trap:** There is no single dedicated regulator for the development industry.
**Mains hook:** Argue that fragmented oversight is a sector accountability weakness.
**Back:** *"Four regulators, no single one."*

### Flashcard GOV-1106 — 2024 GS-II Q6: public charitable trusts and inclusive development
**Front:** What does the 2024 GS-II Q6 ask, and how should the answer be built?
**Definition (learn):** "Public charitable trusts have the potential to make India's development more inclusive as they relate to certain vital public issues. Comment."
**Breakdown:**
- Build: legal and flexibility advantage over routine state bureaucracy; vital-issue examples (health, education, disaster relief); a balanced caveat on accountability, scale and donor-driven risk.
- Conditional answer: the stem asserts potential, not outcome.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Verified transcription (✅ owner 03, 2024 GS-II Q6). Owner 03 notes a routing conflict: the audited 2024-2025 ledger routes this demand to the NGO and civil-society owner (file 04), which solves it there (✅ owner 03).
**Links:** GOV-1097, GOV-1098, GOV-1111 (disaster relief), GOV-1113.
**Trap:** Answering as if potential were realised automatically.
**Mains hook:** Qualify the potential with governance conditions.
**Back:** *"Potential, not promise: conditions decide inclusion."*

### Flashcard GOV-1107 — 2019 GS-II Q16: multi-level planning in the neo-liberal paradigm
**Front:** What does the 2019 GS-II Q16 ask, and what is the answer's spine?
**Definition (learn):** The question asks for a discussion of multi-level planning in the neo-liberal development paradigm (15 marks). Multi-level planning distributes plan formulation across Union, State, district and local tiers. The neo-liberal paradigm favours market allocation, private provision and deregulation, redefining the state as regulator and enabler.
**Breakdown:**
- Spine: multi-level planning presumes public planning authority at each tier; neo-liberal shift can hollow it out.
- Supporting: decentralised planning fits subsidiarity and allocative fit.
- Undercutting: tied grants convert planning into scheme execution; competitive investment pulls decisions upward.
- Verdict: tiers allocate tied resources and coordinate non-state provision rather than set priorities.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Verified transcription (✅ owner 03, 2019 GS-II Q16); word limit 250 words (owner 03 advanced).
**Links:** GOV-1108 (DPC and Kerala), GOV-1085, GOV-1101.
**Trap:** Do not quote a Kerala devolved-share percentage or any outlay figure; owner 03 says not to.
**Mains hook:** Argue both directions, then give a verdict on what planning now decides.
**Back:** *"Neo-liberal: the state moves from producer to enabler; planning shrinks to tied resources."*

### Flashcard GOV-1108 — District Planning Committees (Art. 243ZD) and Kerala's People's Plan Campaign (1996)
**Front:** Which institution is the constitutional consolidation mechanism for district planning, and which Indian example best shows multi-level planning working?
**Definition (learn):** District Planning Committees under Article 243ZD consolidate panchayat and municipal plans. Kerala's People's Plan Campaign (1996) is the strongest Indian demonstration that multi-level planning works when funds, functions and functionaries move together.
**Breakdown:**
- Article 243ZD: District Planning Committee.
- State Finance Commissions: fiscal precondition.
- Kerala People's Plan Campaign: 1996.
- Qualification: DPC operationalisation is uneven.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Article 243ZD and 1996 (✅ owner 03). No devolved-share percentage asserted.
**Links:** GOV-1107, GOV-1050, GOV-1131 (local governance file 12).
**Trap:** Do not claim DPCs work uniformly across States; do not quote a Kerala percentage.
**Mains hook:** Use Kerala as the example and name the funds-functions-functionaries condition.
**Back:** *"243ZD consolidates; Kerala 1996 proves it when funds, functions and staff move together."*

### Flashcard GOV-1109 — 2022 GS-II Q17: donor dependence vs community participation
**Front:** What does the 2022 GS-II Q17 ask, and what thesis does owner 03 defend?
**Definition (learn):** "Dependence on donor agencies and community participation in development. Justify your answer" (15 marks). Owner 03's thesis: donor dependence and community participation are not automatically opposed, but donor funding weakens participation where the cycle, metric and exit are designed around the donor.
**Breakdown:**
- Four channels: agenda, metric, cycle, accountability substitution (GOV-1099).
- Counter-evidence: donor and CSR finance can support mobilisers and SHG federations.
- Five-test grid.
- Correctives: convergence, co-decision in project selection, exit planning, cost disclosure.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Verified transcription (✅ owner 03; 2022 GS-II Q17, 250 words).
**Links:** GOV-1099, GOV-1098, GOV-1094.
**Trap:** Fence-sitting; "justify" requires a position.
**Mains hook:** Argue that dependence is a design property, not an inherent property of external finance.
**Back:** *"Dependence is designed, not inherent: the same rupee can strengthen or displace."*

### Flashcard GOV-1110 — 2025 GS-II Q17: decision-making distant from source of information and execution
**Front:** What does the 2025 GS-II Q17 ask, and which parts of the funding chain does owner 03 cover?
**Definition (learn):** "Decision-making distant from the source of information and execution in contemporary development models. Critically evaluate" (15 marks). Owner 03 answers the development-industry half: distance is created by a funding chain in which the decider (donor board, CSR committee, appraisal team) is remote.
**Breakdown:**
- Decider remote from information and execution.
- Cross-link: file 14 subsidiarity and elite-capture counter-argument.
- Critically evaluate: needs graded judgement.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2025 GS-II Q17 routed to file 03 (✅ owner 03).
**Links:** GOV-1096, GOV-1099, GOV-1107.
**Trap:** Locating distance only in the state; the funding chain also creates it.
**Mains hook:** Trace the decider's distance from the information source.
**Back:** *"Distance grows along the funding chain, not only at the state."*

### Flashcard GOV-1111 — Disaster relief: where charitable trusts and CSR are strongest
**Front:** Why is disaster relief the clearest niche for charitable trusts and CSR?
**Definition (learn):** Disaster relief needs fast, flexible resource deployment that routine state budgeting and procurement cycles provide more slowly.
**Breakdown:**
- Strength: speed and flexibility.
- Limit: discretion makes long-term programmes weakly answerable.
- Sustained development work exposes convergence failures.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 (⚠️ analytical).
**Links:** GOV-1113, GOV-1095, GOV-1114.
**Trap:** The flexibility that serves disaster relief is the same discretion that weakens sustained answerability.
**Mains hook:** Use disaster relief to illustrate complementarity, and then the limit.
**Back:** *"Fast in a crisis, weaker over the long haul."*

### Flashcard GOV-1112 — Democratic-deficit objection to unelected development priorities
**Front:** What is the democratic-deficit objection to development priorities set by donors and foundations?
**Definition (learn):** Development priorities set by unelected boards lack the legitimacy of an elected plan, however well-intentioned they are.
**Breakdown:**
- Source of legitimacy: election vs board appointment.
- Reply: legitimacy can be partly restored through disclosure and beneficiary voice.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 counter-argument bank (⚠️).
**Links:** GOV-1097, GOV-1095, GOV-1108.
**Trap:** Good intentions do not supply electoral legitimacy.
**Mains hook:** Pair the objection with a remedy: disclosure and co-decision.
**Back:** *"Good intent is not a mandate."*

### Flashcard GOV-1113 — Sovereignty and security objection to foreign funding of advocacy
**Front:** What is the sovereignty or security objection to foreign funding, and how should an answer treat it?
**Definition (learn):** Foreign funding of advocacy raises national-security screening concerns. This is the stated rationale for FCRA. Its proportionality is contested.
**Breakdown:**
- Stated rationale: national security screening.
- Contested: proportionality.
- Do not adjudicate a specific case.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 (⚠️ owner-stated rationale; see file 04).
**Links:** GOV-1104, GOV-1106, GOV-1103.
**Trap:** Presenting FCRA's rationale as settled or as proven misuse.
**Mains hook:** State the rationale, then the proportionality question.
**Back:** *"Stated rationale: security. Contested: proportion."*

### Flashcard GOV-1114 — Sustainability: pilot collapse when donor funding ends
**Front:** Why can a successful donor-funded pilot collapse after the grant cycle ends?
**Definition (learn):** Donor and CSR funding is project-based and time-bound. A pilot can fail once funding ends unless it is deliberately transitioned to state ownership.
**Breakdown:**
- Cause: project cycle mismatch.
- Remedy: planned hand-over to state ownership.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 advanced (⚠️).
**Links:** GOV-1098 (sustainability test), GOV-1080 (pilot-to-scale in file 02).
**Trap:** Mistaking a pilot's success for sustained delivery.
**Mains hook:** Demand an exit and hand-over plan at design stage.
**Back:** *"Grant ends, service ends, unless the state takes over."*

### Flashcard GOV-1115 — Section 8 company: profit distribution prohibition
**Front:** What restriction distinguishes a Section 8 company from an ordinary company?
**Definition (learn):** It is prohibited from distributing profits to its members. This distinguishes it from an ordinary for-profit company under the Companies Act 2013.
**Breakdown:**
- Ordinary company: distributes profits.
- Section 8 company: charitable objects; no dividends.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Section 8 (✅ owner 03 advanced).
**Links:** GOV-1089, GOV-1119.
**Trap:** Thinking a Section 8 company cannot earn surpluses; it can, but cannot distribute them to members.
**Mains hook:** Use as a governance-quality point.
**Back:** *"Surplus stays in the mission."*

### Flashcard GOV-1116 — CSR-2 reporting and impact-assessment rules under the CSR Policy Rules
**Front:** Where do CSR-2 reporting and rule-based impact assessment requirements come from?
**Definition (learn):** They derive from the amended Companies (CSR Policy) Rules, not from section 135 alone. Thresholds are set by rule and are revisable.
**Breakdown:**
- CSR-2: filing requirement.
- Impact assessment: rule-based, above prescribed thresholds.
- Cite the rule and reporting year.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 current anchor, status checked 21 July 2026 (✅ owner 03 status; rule text not re-fetched ⚠️).
**Links:** GOV-1090, GOV-1092, GOV-1089.
**Trap:** Quoting a rupee threshold from s.135 alone.
**Mains hook:** Cite the rule and the reporting year.
**Back:** *"CSR-2 comes from the Rules; check the year."*

### Flashcard GOV-1117 — Briefcase NGO risk
**Front:** What is a "briefcase NGO", and why is it a sector-level risk?
**Definition (learn):** A non-genuine organisation registered as an NGO, which can operate within weak and fragmented regulatory gaps.
**Breakdown:**
- Cause: fragmented oversight across MCA, Income Tax, state registrars and MHA.
- Effect: reputational and fund-diversion risk.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 advanced (⚠️ owner-stated risk, no case cited).
**Links:** GOV-1105, GOV-1104.
**Trap:** Labelling any NGO a briefcase NGO without evidence.
**Mains hook:** Argue for a unified verification route as a correction.
**Back:** *"Paper NGO, real fund risk."*

### Flashcard GOV-1118 — Concept card: development industry accountability in one line
**Front:** In one sentence, what is the file 03 concept?
**Definition (learn):** The development industry expands development capacity through flexibility and targeted funding, but its inclusion effect depends on whether donor and trustee accountability meaningfully substitutes for electoral accountability.
**Breakdown:**
- Capacity: flexibility, speed.
- Inclusion: depends on need-fit, disclosure and beneficiary route.
- Accountability: upward, not downward.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 thesis (✅ owner 03).
**Links:** GOV-1085, GOV-1096, GOV-1097, GOV-1098.
**Trap:** Charitable legal form does not supply inclusion.
**Mains hook:** Open with this thesis.
**Back:** *"Capacity without downward answerability is not inclusion."*

### Flashcard GOV-1119 — Comparison: trust vs society vs Section 8 company
**Front:** How do a public charitable trust, a society and a Section 8 company compare on law, governance and external scrutiny?
**Definition (learn):** Three forms with different governing law and accountability.
**Breakdown:**
- Public charitable trust: deed + state law; founder purpose; weakest external governance; regime varies by State.
- Society: 1860 Act or state variants; membership and general body.
- Section 8 company: Companies Act 2013; board; disclosure; no profit distribution.
- Ordering of external scrutiny (owner 03): trust < society < Section 8 company.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 (✅).
**Links:** GOV-1086, GOV-1088, GOV-1089, GOV-1093.
**Trap:** Assuming a trust is subject to company disclosure.
**Mains hook:** Name the form before assessing transparency.
**Back:** *"Trust by deed, society by members, Section 8 by board and disclosure."*

### GROUP — NGOs, SHGs and Civil Society Stakeholders

### Flashcard GOV-1120 — Civil society stakeholders: NGOs, SHGs and other CSOs
**Front:** Which stakeholders make up civil society in owner 04, and what does each do?
**Definition (learn):** Civil-society organisations (CSOs) are non-state bodies. The owner groups them as NGOs (service delivery, advocacy, research), SHGs (women's livelihood collectives) and other CSOs (advocacy, watchdog, pressure groups).
**Breakdown:**
- NGOs: service delivery, advocacy, research.
- SHGs: savings and mutual-action groups, mostly women.
- Other CSOs: advocacy, watchdog, pressure groups (typology is Polity-owned).
- Civil society is wider than NGOs: unions, cooperatives, faith bodies, RWAs, student bodies (see GOV-1165).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 (✅ owner 04).
**Links:** GOV-1121, GOV-1123, GOV-1148, GOV-1165.
**Trap:** Answering a civil-society stem with NGO material alone is a scope error.
**Mains hook:** Disaggregate the actors before judging their role: service deliverer, advocate, watchdog, community institution.
**Back:** *"Civil society is wider than NGOs; disaggregate before judging."*

### Flashcard GOV-1121 — Putnam's social capital typology: bonding, bridging, linking
**Front:** What are the three types of social capital in Putnam's typology, and what governance function does each serve?
**Definition (learn):** Social capital is networks, norms of reciprocity and trust that enable collective action. Putnam's typology distinguishes bonding (within a homogenous group), bridging (across groups) and linking (between citizens and formal institutions, including the state).
**Breakdown:**
- Bonding: internal trust within an SHG savings circle.
- Bridging: across SHGs through federations (scale, negotiation).
- Linking: group to bank, market or department (credit, price, scheme access).
- None substitutes for the others.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Putnam typology (✅ owner 04).
**Links:** GOV-1120, GOV-1151 (empowerment chain), GOV-1175 (capture trap), GOV-1154 (PYQ 2025 Q6).
**Trap:** Bonding without bridging can entrench in-group exclusion.
**Mains hook:** Structure any SHG or civil-society empowerment answer with bonding → bridging → linking.
**Back:** *"Bond inside, bridge across, link upward."*

### Flashcard GOV-1122 — Trap: social capital is not financial capital
**Front:** Are social capital and financial capital the same thing?
**Definition (learn):** No. Social capital is networks, trust and reciprocity. It can help access financial capital but is conceptually distinct.
**Breakdown:**
- Social capital: networks, trust, reciprocity.
- Financial capital: money, credit, assets.
- Link: social capital can facilitate access to credit.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 (✅).
**Links:** GOV-1121, GOV-1158 (credit as empowerment).
**Trap:** Quoting loan access as proof of social capital, or the reverse.
**Mains hook:** Separate network trust from the money it may unlock.
**Back:** *"Trust is social, cash is financial: keep them apart."*

### Flashcard GOV-1123 — NGO is a purpose or practice, not one legal form
**Front:** Is "NGO" a single legal form?
**Definition (learn):** No. NGO describes a nonprofit purpose or practice. An NGO may be a public charitable trust, a registered society or a Section 8 company. An SHG, cooperative, union or informal movement is not automatically an NGO.
**Breakdown:**
- Trust: deed and state law.
- Society: Societies Registration Act, 1860 or state variant.
- Section 8 company: Companies Act, 2013.
- Not NGOs by default: SHGs, cooperatives, unions, informal movements.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 (✅).
**Links:** GOV-1093 (file 03), GOV-1119, GOV-1124.
**Trap:** Using "NGO" as the common incorporation statute.
**Mains hook:** Name the legal form and its regulator before judging accountability.
**Back:** *"NGO is a purpose; the form is a deed, a society or a company."*

### Flashcard GOV-1124 — Self-Help Group (SHG): small voluntary savings group, mostly women
**Front:** What is a Self-Help Group, and is it an NGO or a bank?
**Definition (learn):** A small, voluntary savings and mutual-action group, predominantly of women in the Indian livelihoods context. It may obtain bank linkage and join a federation, but is not itself a bank, MFI, NGO or incorporated company.
**Breakdown:**
- Basis: savings and mutual action.
- Membership: mostly women.
- Can: obtain bank linkage, join a federation.
- Is not: a bank, MFI, NGO or company.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 (✅).
**Links:** GOV-1125 (NABARD SHG-BLP), GOV-1126 (DAY-NRLM), GOV-1127 (DAY-NULM).
**Trap:** An SHG is not an NGO by definition.
**Mains hook:** Use the SHG as the base of social capital, not as a credit institution.
**Back:** *"Savings first, members mostly women, not a bank."*

### Flashcard GOV-1125 — NABARD's SHG-Bank Linkage Programme (SHG-BLP)
**Front:** What is the SHG-Bank Linkage Programme, who evolved it, and what did it start from?
**Definition (learn):** A savings-led model that links SHGs to banks, evolved by NABARD from its 1992-93 pilot. NABARD facilitates through policy, promotion, capacity building, SHG-promoting institutions and refinance. Banks provide group accounts and loans.
**Breakdown:**
- Originator: NABARD.
- Start: 1992-93 pilot.
- NABARD role: policy, promotion, capacity, refinance.
- Bank role: accounts and loans.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1992-93 pilot (✅ owner 04; NABARD microcredit page cited in owner 04). Not re-fetched in full (⚠️).
**Links:** GOV-1124, GOV-1126, GOV-1129 (trap).
**Trap:** NABARD is not the retail lender to every SHG.
**Mains hook:** Credit access is not enterprise viability; test income effects.
**Back:** *"NABARD built the bank link in 1992-93; banks lend."*

### Flashcard GOV-1126 — DAY-NRLM: rural SHG → Village Organisation → Cluster-Level Federation
**Front:** What is DAY-NRLM, which ministry runs it, and what is its federation ladder?
**Definition (learn):** Deendayal Antyodaya Yojana - National Rural Livelihoods Mission, run by the Ministry of Rural Development. It mobilises rural women into SHGs and federates them into village organisations and cluster-level federations.
**Breakdown:**
- Ministry: Ministry of Rural Development (MoRD).
- Ladder: SHG → Village Organisation (VO) → Cluster-Level Federation (CLF).
- Support: revolving fund, community investment, livelihood, bank linkage.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 (✅). No SHG or household total asserted (❌ not carried).
**Links:** GOV-1125, GOV-1127, GOV-1128 (CLF trap), GOV-1168 (2026 coordination meeting).
**Trap:** DAY-NRLM uses bank linkage; it did not originate SHG-BLP.
**Mains hook:** Use the three-tier rural ladder to show scale.
**Back:** *"Rural: SHG, village, cluster — Ministry of Rural Development."*

### Flashcard GOV-1127 — DAY-NULM: urban SHG → Area Level Federation → City Level Federation
**Front:** What is DAY-NULM, which ministry runs it, and what is its federation ladder?
**Definition (learn):** Deendayal Antyodaya Yojana - National Urban Livelihoods Mission, under the Ministry of Housing and Urban Affairs (MoHUA). Urban SHGs federate into Area Level Federations and City Level Federations.
**Breakdown:**
- Ministry: MoHUA.
- Ladder: SHG → Area Level Federation (ALF) → City Level Federation (CLF).
- Resource organisations may facilitate formation and bank linkage.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 (✅); DAY-NULM mission document cited in owner 04.
**Links:** GOV-1126, GOV-1128, GOV-1129.
**Trap:** The urban CLF is a City Level Federation above an ALF, not the rural Cluster-Level Federation.
**Mains hook:** Full urban-poverty architecture is Social Justice-owned (file 16).
**Back:** *"Urban: SHG, area, city — Housing and Urban Affairs."*

### Flashcard GOV-1128 — Trap: "CLF" means different institutions in DAY-NRLM and DAY-NULM
**Front:** What does "CLF" mean in DAY-NRLM and in DAY-NULM?
**Definition (learn):** In DAY-NRLM it is the Cluster-Level Federation. In DAY-NULM it is the City Level Federation, which sits above an Area Level Federation.
**Breakdown:**
- Rural: Cluster-Level Federation.
- Urban: City Level Federation.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 (✅).
**Links:** GOV-1126, GOV-1127.
**Trap:** Assuming the two CLFs are the same tier.
**Mains hook:** Always expand the abbreviation with the mission name.
**Back:** *"Same letters, two missions: cluster rural, city urban."*

### Flashcard GOV-1129 — Trap: NABARD, DAY-NRLM and DAY-NULM are not three names for one programme
**Front:** Are NABARD, DAY-NRLM and DAY-NULM three names for one SHG programme?
**Definition (learn):** No. NABARD evolved and supports SHG-BLP; DAY-NRLM is the rural MoRD livelihoods architecture; DAY-NULM is the urban MoHUA architecture. They overlap in practice but answer different institutional questions.
**Breakdown:**
- NABARD: SHG-BLP origin and facilitation.
- DAY-NRLM: rural community institutions.
- DAY-NULM: urban community institutions.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 (✅).
**Links:** GOV-1125, GOV-1126, GOV-1127.
**Trap:** Combining them into one "government SHG scheme" hides origin, ministry, geography and role.
**Mains hook:** Name the ministry and the origin for each route.
**Back:** *"Three bodies, three roles: NABARD lends the model, two ministries run missions."*

### Flashcard GOV-1130 — FCRA, 2010: controlling Act, MHA administration
**Front:** Which Act regulates foreign contributions, which ministry administers it, and what is its current amendment status?
**Definition (learn):** The Foreign Contribution (Regulation) Act, 2010, substantially amended in 2020, regulates foreign contributions through registration or source-and-purpose-specific prior permission, renewal, accounts, records and reporting. Administered by the Ministry of Home Affairs (MHA).
**Breakdown:**
- Act: FCRA 2010, amended 2020.
- Rules: FCRA Rules, 2011, as amended (2024, 2025 and 23 June 2026).
- Administrator: MHA.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** FCRA 2010 amended 2020; Rules amended through 23 June 2026 (✅ owner 04; official FCRA portal cited).
**Links:** GOV-1131, GOV-1132, GOV-1137, GOV-1138.
**Trap:** Act and Rules have different amendment dates; do not merge them.
**Mains hook:** Foreign-funding gate with stated security rationale and contested proportionality.
**Back:** *"Two thousand ten, amended twenty; rules keep moving."*

### Flashcard GOV-1131 — FCRA timeline: assent, commencement and 2020 amendment votes
**Front:** When did the FCRA 2010 receive assent and take effect, and when did the 2020 amendment pass?
**Definition (learn):** The 2010 Act received presidential assent on 26 September 2010 and took effect on 1 May 2011. The 2020 amendment was passed by the Lok Sabha on 21 September 2020 and by the Rajya Sabha on 23 September 2020.
**Breakdown:**
- Assent: 26 September 2010.
- Commencement: 1 May 2011.
- 2020 amendment: Lok Sabha 21 September 2020; Rajya Sabha 23 September 2020.
- The 1976 predecessor law is mentioned by Wikipedia.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Dates ✅ Wikipedia (secondary source, fetched) and consistent with owner 04's "2010 Act amended 2020". Owner 04 does not state these days; confirm against the Gazette before examining. Wikipedia's notice warns of possible LLM text (⚠️).
**Links:** GOV-1130, GOV-1132.
**Trap:** Do not confuse the 2010 Act's commencement with the 2020 amendment's passage.
**Mains hook:** Cite the statutory timeline as the baseline for any FCRA reform argument.
**Back:** *"Assent '10, commenced '11, amended in 2020's September."*

### Flashcard GOV-1132 — FCRA: registration vs prior permission, and five-year validity
**Front:** What is the difference between FCRA registration and prior permission?
**Definition (learn):** Registration permits continuing receipts, subject to renewal. Prior permission is specific to a source and purpose. The certificate is ordinarily valid for five years, with renewal sought within the statutory pre-expiry window.
**Breakdown:**
- Registration: continuing receipts, five-year validity, renewal.
- Prior permission: one source, one purpose.
- Renewal: file early; certificate may cease on expiry while late renewal is pending (MHA notice 30 September 2025, owner 04 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Five-year validity (✅ owner 04). MHA renewal notice 30 September 2025 (✅ owner 04).
**Links:** GOV-1130, GOV-1133, GOV-1169.
**Trap:** Registration and prior permission are not one permit; neither gives tax exemption.
**Mains hook:** Specify which route the organisation uses before judging its foreign funding.
**Back:** *"Register for continuing receipts, permit for one source and purpose."*

### Flashcard GOV-1133 — FCRA receipt account and utilisation accounts: SBI, New Delhi Main Branch
**Front:** Where must foreign contribution first be received, and what other accounts may be opened?
**Definition (learn):** Foreign contribution must first be received in the designated FCRA Account at SBI, New Delhi Main Branch. Another FCRA account and one or more utilisation accounts may be opened in scheduled banks for keeping or utilising transferred foreign contribution. Non-foreign-contribution funds cannot be mixed in.
**Breakdown:**
- Receipt account: SBI, New Delhi Main Branch (designated).
- Utilisation accounts: scheduled banks, after transfer.
- Rule: no non-foreign-contribution money in these accounts.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** SBI New Delhi Main Branch (✅ owner 04). Wikipedia did not confirm the bank or branch (⚠️).
**Links:** GOV-1130, GOV-1134, GOV-1136.
**Trap:** Receipt account is not the same as the utilisation account.
**Mains hook:** Account separation is the traceability control for foreign money.
**Back:** *"Receive at SBI New Delhi, use elsewhere, mix nothing."*

### Flashcard GOV-1134 — FC-4 annual return: nine months after financial-year closure
**Front:** What is Form FC-4, and what is its deadline?
**Definition (learn):** Form FC-4 is the annual FCRA reporting return, filed within nine months of financial-year closure, with prescribed financial statements and current project, activity and location disclosures.
**Breakdown:**
- Deadline: within nine months of financial-year closure.
- Contents: prescribed accounts, project, activity and location disclosures.
- 2025 Rules added project, activity and location-wise FC-4 reporting (owner 04 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Nine months (✅ owner 04).
**Links:** GOV-1133, GOV-1136.
**Trap:** FC-4 is not a registration; late filing is a compliance risk.
**Mains hook:** Reporting is traceability, not a proof of impact.
**Back:** *"FC-4 within nine months, with every project named."*

### Flashcard GOV-1135 — FCRA administrative expense cap: 20% of annual foreign contribution (owner-only)
**Front:** What cap applies to administrative expenses of foreign contribution?
**Definition (learn):** Owner 04 advanced states that administrative expenses are ordinarily capped at 20% of annual foreign contribution without prior Central Government approval.
**Breakdown:**
- Cap: 20% of annual foreign contribution (owner-stated).
- Exception: prior Central Government approval.
- 2024 Rules allow reasoned carry-forward of an unspent part of the allowable amount (owner 04).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 20% (✅ owner 04 advanced only). Wikipedia did not state the figure (⚠️ not independently verified).
**Links:** GOV-1133, GOV-1136.
**Trap:** Do not present the 20% as a universal limit without the exception.
**Mains hook:** Admin caps limit overhead, but also constrain capacity building.
**Back:** *"Overhead twenty per cent, unless the Centre says otherwise."*

### Flashcard GOV-1136 — FCRA Rules updates: 2024, 2025 and 23 June 2026
**Front:** What did the 2024, 2025 and 23 June 2026 FCRA Rules change?
**Definition (learn):** The Rules have been amended in these three steps. Each strengthens a different documentation or reporting layer.
**Breakdown:**
- 2024 Rules: reasoned carry-forward of an unspent part of the administrative-expense amount.
- 2025 Rules: strengthened registration, prior-permission, renewal, bank-account and FC-4 documentation.
- 23 June 2026 Rules: purpose and territorial requirements, renewal and reporting.
- Owner 04 advanced: a renewal-utilisation condition of at least ₹10 lakh in foreign contribution over the preceding two years, per PIB FAQ (⚠️ not re-fetched).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Rule dates (✅ owner 04, official FCRA portal). ₹10 lakh condition (✅ owner 04 citing PIB FAQ, 22 July 2026).
**Links:** GOV-1130, GOV-1137, GOV-1134.
**Trap:** The 2026 Rules are not the 2026 Bill.
**Mains hook:** Rules change faster than Acts; cite the amendment date.
**Back:** *"Twenty-four carried, twenty-five documented, twenty-six purposed."*

### Flashcard GOV-1137 — FCRA Bill 2026: proposal, not law
**Front:** Is the separately discussed 2026 FCRA amendment Bill current law?
**Definition (learn):** No. A separately discussed 2026 Bill remains a proposal unless and until it is enacted and commenced. The PIB FAQ dated 22 July 2026 labels it proposed.
**Breakdown:**
- Proposal: no legal effect.
- Enactment and commencement: required before it applies.
- Owner 04: teach the Act, the Rules and the Bill as three separate statuses.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PIB FAQ 22 July 2026 (✅ owner 04). Wikipedia notes a 2026 amendment bill allowing cancellations and suspensions; it gives no duration (⚠️ not enacted status).
**Links:** GOV-1136, GOV-1130.
**Trap:** Teaching a proposal as current law.
**Mains hook:** Separate proposal, enactment, commencement and rules before any claim.
**Back:** *"Bill is a proposal until enacted and commenced."*

### Flashcard GOV-1138 — Trap: FCRA regulates foreign contributions, not all NGO funding
**Front:** Does FCRA regulate all NGO funding?
**Definition (learn):** No. FCRA specifically regulates foreign contributions. CSR is a Companies Act route and is not automatically FCRA permission or prohibition.
**Breakdown:**
- FCRA: foreign source contributions.
- CSR: Companies Act s.135 route.
- Verify the statutory "foreign source" definition rather than deciding from the CSR label.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 (✅).
**Links:** GOV-1130, GOV-1138.
**Trap:** Domestic funding is outside FCRA; foreign funding is inside, whatever the label.
**Mains hook:** Check the source before deciding FCRA applies.
**Back:** *"FCRA follows the foreign source, not the label."*

### Flashcard GOV-1139 — Trap: FCRA registration does not confer tax exemption
**Front:** Does FCRA registration give tax-exemption status?
**Definition (learn):** No. Tax exemption (12A) and donor deduction (80G) are separate Income Tax Department processes, independent of FCRA.
**Breakdown:**
- FCRA: foreign funding.
- 12A/80G: tax status (Income Tax Department).
- Three regulatory layers: NGO Darpan, FCRA, 12A/80G.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 (✅).
**Links:** GOV-1145, GOV-1150.
**Trap:** A "registered NGO" is not a tax-exempt NGO by default.
**Mains hook:** Name the layer that gives the permission in question.
**Back:** *"FCRA is funding, 12A is tax: two different doors."*

### Flashcard GOV-1140 — Section 135 CSR thresholds: ₹500 crore net worth, ₹1,000 crore turnover, ₹5 crore net profit
**Front:** What are the three alternative thresholds for CSR applicability under section 135?
**Definition (learn):** CSR applies where any one immediately preceding year threshold is met: net worth at least ₹500 crore, turnover at least ₹1,000 crore, or net profit at least ₹5 crore.
**Breakdown:**
- Net worth: ₹500 crore.
- Turnover: ₹1,000 crore.
- Net profit: ₹5 crore.
- Alternatives, not cumulative (owner 04).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Thresholds (✅ owner 04). Companies (CSR Policy) Amendment Rules, 2021 (G.S.R. 40(E)) cited; thresholds not changed by later rules, per owner 04 (not re-fetched ⚠️).
**Links:** GOV-1141, GOV-1116 (file 03), GOV-1142.
**Trap:** Thresholds are alternatives. Do not add them together.
**Mains hook:** Quote the year of the rule before the threshold.
**Back:** *"Five hundred, one thousand, five: any one triggers CSR."*

### Flashcard GOV-1141 — CSR spending floor: 2% of average net profits of three preceding years
**Front:** What is the CSR spending floor under section 135?
**Definition (learn):** At least 2% of the average net profits of the three immediately preceding financial years, or the available preceding years for a newer company.
**Breakdown:**
- Basis: average net profits.
- Years: three immediately preceding financial years.
- Newer company: available preceding years.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2% (✅ owner 04).
**Links:** GOV-1140, GOV-1090 (file 03).
**Trap:** Basing CSR on current-year profit alone.
**Mains hook:** Use the averaging rule to explain CSR volatility.
**Back:** *"Two per cent of the average, three years back."*

### Flashcard GOV-1142 — CSR implementing agencies: Rule 4 categories
**Front:** Which entities may implement CSR under Rule 4 of the Companies (CSR Policy) Rules?
**Definition (learn):** Four categories: (i) specified Section 8 companies, registered public trusts or registered societies established by the company, with 12A/80G status; (ii) such entities established by the Central or State Government; (iii) statutory entities; (iv) qualifying independent Section 8, trust or society bodies with 12A/80G status and a three-year track record.
**Breakdown:**
- (i) company-established bodies with 12A/80G.
- (ii) government-established bodies.
- (iii) statutory entities.
- (iv) independent bodies with 12A/80G and three-year track record.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Rule 4 categories (✅ owner 04; Companies (CSR Policy) Amendment Rules 2021 cited).
**Links:** GOV-1143, GOV-1140, GOV-1145.
**Trap:** Not every charitable body qualifies; check the category.
**Mains hook:** CSR eligibility is category-specific, not charity-based.
**Back:** *"Company-made, government-made, statutory, or proven independent."*

### Flashcard GOV-1143 — CSR-1 registration: not incorporation, tax approval or FCRA
**Front:** What does Form CSR-1 do, and what does it not do?
**Definition (learn):** Implementing entities file Form CSR-1 where the Rules require it. It registers an eligible CSR implementing entity for that route.
**Breakdown:**
- Does: registers eligibility for CSR implementation.
- Does not: incorporate, approve tax status, give FCRA permission or prove project impact.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 (✅).
**Links:** GOV-1142, GOV-1145, GOV-1150.
**Trap:** CSR-1 proves nothing about delivery quality.
**Mains hook:** Separate CSR eligibility from impact.
**Back:** *"CSR-1 is a CSR door, not a licence to do everything."*

### Flashcard GOV-1144 — NGO Darpan: NITI Aayog identification portal for Union grants
**Front:** What is NGO Darpan, which body runs it, and what is it used for?
**Definition (learn):** NITI Aayog's identification and database portal. It assigns a Unique ID used in covered Union government grant processes and linked interfaces.
**Breakdown:**
- Runs: NITI Aayog.
- Gives: Unique ID.
- Used: covered Union grants.
- Not: legal constitution, grant sanction, CSR-1, FCRA or tax approval.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 (✅ owner 04; NGO Darpan rechecked with MCA on 5 September 2026 per file 03 note).
**Links:** GOV-1145, GOV-1150, GOV-1171 (light-touch).
**Trap:** Darpan is not FCRA registration.
**Mains hook:** Darpan improves visibility; it does not establish financial accountability.
**Back:** *"Darpan identifies for grants; it does not permit or tax."*

### Flashcard GOV-1145 — Trap: NGO Darpan registration is not FCRA registration
**Front:** Is NGO Darpan registration legally identical to FCRA registration?
**Definition (learn):** No. Darpan is a NITI Aayog identification portal relevant mainly to government grants. FCRA is an MHA legal requirement specifically for foreign funding.
**Breakdown:**
- Darpan: NITI Aayog; grant identity.
- FCRA: MHA; foreign funding permission.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 (✅).
**Links:** GOV-1144, GOV-1130, GOV-1150.
**Trap:** Quoting a Darpan ID as foreign funding permission.
**Mains hook:** Name the regulator of each permission.
**Back:** *"Darpan is grants, FCRA is foreign money."*

### Flashcard GOV-1146 — Pressure group: influence policy without forming government
**Front:** What is a pressure group, and what is its governance role?
**Definition (learn):** An organised interest that seeks to influence public policy without itself seeking to form the government. Its governance role is representation, agenda-setting, expertise and scrutiny. Risks: unequal access, opaque lobbying and policy capture.
**Breakdown:**
- Methods: representation, research, petitions, campaigns, consultation, litigation.
- Risks: unequal access, opaque lobbying, capture.
- Composition and typology: Polity-owned (advanced/44).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 (✅).
**Links:** GOV-1147, GOV-1153 (R-I-S-K), GOV-1156.
**Trap:** Pressure groups are not political parties.
**Mains hook:** Evaluate a pressure group by transparency, representativeness, evidence and capture safeguards.
**Back:** *"Influence the policy, don't win the office."*

### Flashcard GOV-1147 — Trap: pressure groups are not political parties
**Front:** Are pressure groups and political parties identical?
**Definition (learn):** No. Pressure groups influence policy without contesting for governmental power as their defining purpose.
**Breakdown:**
- Pressure group: policy influence.
- Party: contests power and forms government.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 (✅).
**Links:** GOV-1146.
**Trap:** Describing a pressure group as a party-in-waiting.
**Mains hook:** Use the purpose test to classify actors.
**Back:** *"Groups shape policy; parties seek power."*

### Flashcard GOV-1148 — Non-state vs anti-State: structural category vs political label
**Front:** How does a non-state actor differ from an "anti-State" actor?
**Definition (learn):** Non-state is a structural or legal category: the CSO is not part of government. Anti-State is a political or perceptual label applied when advocacy, funding or activity is seen as opposing state policy or sovereignty.
**Breakdown:**
- Non-state: legal fact.
- Anti-State: political judgement applied selectively.
- Trigger: advocacy against a specific policy; funding-transparency concerns.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 (⚠️ analytical distinction, grounded in PYQ 2025 Q8).
**Links:** GOV-1149, GOV-1152 (suspicion chain), GOV-1155 (PYQ 2025 Q8).
**Trap:** Treating the two as synonyms.
**Mains hook:** Say the distinction in sentence one.
**Back:** *"Non-state is structure; anti-State is a verdict."*

### Flashcard GOV-1149 — Trap: all civil society organisations are not anti-State by definition
**Front:** Are CSOs "anti-State" by definition?
**Definition (learn):** No. CSOs are non-state actors by definition. Adversarial perception arises from specific advocacy or policy friction, or funding-transparency concerns, not from their non-state status.
**Breakdown:**
- Definition: non-state.
- Perception: triggered by advocacy friction, funding transparency.
- Resolution: contest or justify case by case.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 (✅).
**Links:** GOV-1148, GOV-1152.
**Trap:** A blanket label is a category error.
**Mains hook:** Refuse the blanket label; engage the case.
**Back:** *"Blanket labels are a category error."*

### Flashcard GOV-1150 — Five regulatory layers for NGOs: constitution, FCRA, tax, CSR, grant identity
**Front:** Which five regulatory layers does owner 04 separate for NGOs, and which authority controls each?
**Definition (learn):** Legal constitution (State trust or deed framework; Registrar of Societies; Registrar of Companies); foreign contribution (MHA, FCRA); tax status (Income Tax Department, 12A/80G); CSR implementing status (Companies Act s.135, CSR Rules, MCA/ROC); government-grant identity (NITI Aayog, NGO Darpan).
**Breakdown:**
- Constitution: state trust, society registrar, company registrar.
- Foreign contribution: MHA.
- Tax: Income Tax Department.
- CSR: MCA.
- Grant identity: NITI Aayog.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 (✅).
**Links:** GOV-1103 (file 03), GOV-1139, GOV-1143, GOV-1145.
**Trap:** Treating one "NGO registration" as covering all five.
**Mains hook:** Name the layer before the permission.
**Back:** *"Constitute, fund abroad, tax, CSR, grant ID: five layers, five regulators."*

### Flashcard GOV-1151 — Empowerment chain: SHG savings to gender-equity outcome
**Front:** Trace the SHG empowerment chain set by owner 04.
**Definition (learn):** Savings and meeting discipline → bonding (trust, peer accountability) → federation into VO or CLF → bridging (risk pooling, scale, negotiation) → bank, market or department → linking (credit, price, scheme access) → income control and public voice → gender-equity outcome.
**Breakdown:**
- Failure point: any stage missing, or elite capture at bridging.
- Outcome evidence: control of income, leadership distribution, debt sustainability, actual influence.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 causal chain (⚠️ analytical).
**Links:** GOV-1121, GOV-1154, GOV-1164 (elite capture).
**Trap:** Membership or aggregate credit alone is not an outcome measure.
**Mains hook:** Test each stage with evidence.
**Back:** *"Save, bond, bridge, link, control income: each stage must hold."*

### Flashcard GOV-1152 — Suspicion chain: from watchdog advocacy to "anti-State" label
**Front:** Trace the suspicion chain by which a structurally non-state CSO becomes labelled "anti-State".
**Definition (learn):** Watchdog or advocacy function, structurally independent → advocacy opposes a specific policy or project → perceived as adversarial, not as feedback → foreign funding compounds the perception (FCRA security rationale) → "anti-State" label.
**Breakdown:**
- Resolution: contest or justify case by case; refuse the blanket label.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 causal chain (⚠️ analytical).
**Links:** GOV-1148, GOV-1155, GOV-1137.
**Trap:** The label is applied after the advocacy, not before.
**Mains hook:** Show where the chain breaks in a specific case.
**Back:** *"Watch, oppose, get labelled: the chain breaks at the label."*

### Flashcard GOV-1153 — R-I-S-K: four functions for assessing a pressure group
**Front:** What do the letters R-I-S-K stand for in assessing a pressure group?
**Definition (learn):** Representation, information and expertise, scrutiny, and capture risk.
**Breakdown:**
- R: representation of interests.
- I: information and expertise.
- S: scrutiny of government.
- K: capture risk.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 PYQ application (✅ owner 04).
**Links:** GOV-1146, GOV-1157, GOV-1156.
**Trap:** Listing organisations without evaluating them.
**Mains hook:** Use R-I-S-K to assess a named group.
**Back:** *"Represent, inform, scrutinise, beware capture."*

### Flashcard GOV-1154 — 2025 GS-II Q6: women's social capital and empowerment
**Front:** What does the 2025 GS-II Q6 ask, and how should the answer sequence it?
**Definition (learn):** "Women's social capital complements in advancing empowerment and gender equity. Explain." Build with bonding, bridging and linking, each tied to economic agency and public voice.
**Breakdown:**
- Bonding: internal SHG trust and savings.
- Bridging: federation scale.
- Linking: bank, market and government access.
- Outcome: empowerment and gender equity, tested by control and voice.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Verified transcription (✅ owner 04; 2025 GS-II Q6). Primary ownership: Social Justice file 05.
**Links:** GOV-1121, GOV-1151, GOV-1158.
**Trap:** A generic "SHGs help women" statement loses marks.
**Mains hook:** Sequence the three forms of capital and test each with an outcome.
**Back:** *"Bond, bridge, link: each one a different empowerment."*

### Flashcard GOV-1155 — 2025 GS-II Q8: CSOs as anti-State or non-State actors
**Front:** What does the 2025 GS-II Q8 ask, and what position is defensible?
**Definition (learn):** "Civil Society Organizations are often perceived as being anti-State actors rather than non-State actors. Do you agree? Justify." Defensible position: CSOs are structurally non-state; anti-State perception arises from advocacy friction or funding transparency, not an inherent character.
**Breakdown:**
- Commit in the introduction.
- Support: structural independence.
- Limit: advocacy friction and FCRA-related funding transparency.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Verified transcription (✅ owner 04; 2025 GS-II Q8, 10 marks, 150 words).
**Links:** GOV-1148, GOV-1149, GOV-1152.
**Trap:** A one-sided "always benign" or "always adversarial" answer.
**Mains hook:** Take a position and qualify it with one supporting and one limiting case.
**Back:** *"Structurally non-state; perceived anti-State by friction, not by nature."*

### Flashcard GOV-1156 — 2025 GS-II Q15: environmental pressure groups (Polity-owned)
