**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ❌ No cumulative DBT savings figure is asserted in the owner or this card (✅ owner 13 §5, §7, §13.10).
**Links:** GOV-3043, GOV-3042.
**Trap:** A cumulative savings total is a dated, changing figure, not a permanent citation.
**Mains hook:** The durable proposition is the mechanism (removed fake and duplicate entries), not the total.
**Back:** *"Savings count the ghosts removed; welfare counts the people still missing."*

### Flashcard GOV-3045 — PFMS (Public Financial Management System)
**Front:** What does PFMS do, and under which ministry does it sit?
**Definition (learn):** PFMS tracks government fund flow from budgetary sanction through release to implementing agencies and actual expenditure or utilisation, across schemes.
**Breakdown:**
- Administered by the Department of Expenditure, Ministry of Finance (✅ owner 13 §2).
- Covers government-to-agency and government-to-implementer flows, not only beneficiary payments.
- Includes sanction and payment reporting and "Know Your Payment" (⚠️ owner 13 §5; not every internal utilisation trail is public).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** PFMS under Department of Expenditure (✅ owner 13 §4; verified against pfms.nic.in by owner 13 on 5 Sept 2026 per the owner's semantic ledger).
**Links:** GOV-3046, GOV-3047, GOV-3041, GOV-3050.
**Trap:** PFMS and DBT are not the same system (✅ owner 13 §7).
**Mains hook:** Cite PFMS as the fund-flow transparency layer, distinct from DBT's beneficiary-transfer layer.
**Back:** *"PFMS follows the rupee from sanction to spend, not to the beneficiary's hand."*

### Flashcard GOV-3046 — SNA model (Single Nodal Agency), 2021
**Front:** What is the SNA model, and to which schemes does it apply?
**Definition (learn):** A designated agency and account structure for centrally sponsored schemes, routing their state-level fund flow through a single state-level bank account per scheme.
**Breakdown:**
- Improved visibility over balances held in State accounts.
- Applies to designated centrally sponsored schemes, not all Union-to-State transfers.
- Finance Commission grants run under a different mechanism (✅ owner 13 advanced §8).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** SNA model 2021 (✅ owner 13 §4 and §13.4).
**Links:** GOV-3047, GOV-3048, GOV-3045.
**Trap:** The bank-account SNA model is no longer the whole current architecture; SNA-SPARSH supersedes it in design (❌ owner 13 §2).
**Mains hook:** Use SNA as the Union's fiscal-discipline architecture for CSS fund flow.
**Back:** *"One nodal account per scheme, for the Centre's shared schemes only."*

### Flashcard GOV-3047 — SNA-SPARSH: just-in-time release
**Front:** What is SNA-SPARSH, and what does it integrate?
**Definition (learn):** Department of Expenditure's current just-in-time CSS release architecture. It releases the Centre and State shares together against actual expenditure claims, rather than parking money in advance.
**Breakdown:**
- Integrates PFMS, State IFMIS and RBI e-Kuber.
- Targets float and idle parking in CSS release.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** SNA-SPARSH is the current official architecture (✅ owner 13 §8).
**Links:** GOV-3046, GOV-3048, GOV-3049, GOV-3061.
**Trap:** "Operational in all States" is not what the notification establishes; say "applicable from" a date (✅ owner 13 §13.10).
**Mains hook:** Just-in-time release is a fiscal-efficiency gain paid for by State cash-planning flexibility and a reconciliation load.
**Back:** *"SPARSH: release when spent, not parked in advance."*

### Flashcard GOV-3048 — SNA-SPARSH timeline (OM dates)
**Front:** What are the key dates in the SNA-SPARSH roll-out?
**Definition (learn):** A chronology of Department of Expenditure instruments.
**Breakdown:**
- 13 July 2023: introduced by Department of Expenditure OM No. 1(27)/PFMS/2020.
- 10 June 2025: OM extended SNA-SPARSH to 37 further CSSs from 1 July 2025.
- 1 November 2025: applies to all CSSs in States and UTs with legislatures.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 13 §8 and §13.10 (📰 verified 13 August 2026 by owner; applicability by notification, not verified operational onboarding for every scheme).
**Links:** GOV-3047, GOV-3046.
**Trap:** Dates are of the OMs and applicability, not of universal operational integration.
**Mains hook:** Cite the dated sequence and then flag onboarding and reconciliation as the implementation test.
**Back:** *"Two-thousand-twenty-three to twenty-five: one OM, one extension, one full sweep."*

### Flashcard GOV-3049 — RBI e-Kuber
**Front:** What role does RBI e-Kuber play in fund release?
**Definition (learn):** RBI's core banking system used for government transactions. In SNA-SPARSH it settles transfers between the Centre's and the State's RBI accounts without commercial-bank float.
**Breakdown:**
- Infrastructure, not policy.
- Part of the PFMS-State IFMIS-e-Kuber integration.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 13 §4 and §13.4.
**Links:** GOV-3047, GOV-3048.
**Trap:** e-Kuber is an RBI settlement platform, not a Department of Expenditure scheme.
**Mains hook:** Settlement without float is the mechanism of just-in-time release.
**Back:** *"e-Kuber settles, SPARSH schedules."*

### Flashcard GOV-3050 — General Financial Rules, 2017
**Front:** What are the General Financial Rules, 2017, and what do they govern?
**Definition (learn):** The Union government's consolidated rulebook for financial management, budgeting, procurement and expenditure control across ministries and departments.
**Breakdown:**
- Ex-ante procedural rules: sanctioning powers, procurement procedure, expenditure classifications.
- Comprehensively revised in 2017; periodically amended since.
- Rules, not outcomes.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** GFR comprehensively revised 2017 (✅ owner 13 §4, §6).
**Links:** GOV-3053, GOV-3051, GOV-3039.
**Trap:** Rules discipline process; they do not produce value by themselves (✅ owner 13 T4).
**Mains hook:** GFR is the procedural backbone, not a separate reform.
**Back:** *"Twenty-seventeen rulebook: the process is clean; the outcome needs more."*

### Flashcard GOV-3051 — Outcome budgeting
**Front:** What is outcome budgeting, and how does it differ from input and output budgeting?
**Definition (learn):** Outcome budgeting links allocation to measurable outcome targets, rather than only tracking money spent or physical units produced.
**Breakdown:**
- Input budgeting: how much is allocated to a category.
- Output budgeting: physical units produced per allocation.
- Outcome budgeting: change achieved relative to allocation.
- Output-Outcome Monitoring Framework is the operational form (see GOV-3065 and the M&E group).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 13 §13.4; ⚠️ owner 13 §3 (analytical).
**Links:** GOV-3039, GOV-3050, and M&E group cards.
**Trap:** Outcome targets can themselves be set undemandingly; a stated outcome is not an achieved outcome.
**Mains hook:** Outcome budgeting shifts the budget conversation from spend to effect, but outcome achievement carries no budgetary consequence (owner 15 verdict).
**Back:** *"Input counts money, output counts things, outcome counts change."*

### Flashcard GOV-3052 — Procurement under GFR: beyond corruption
**Front:** What failure points beyond corruption must a procurement answer cover?
**Definition (learn):** Procurement by Union ministries operates within GFR 2017 and procurement manuals; the objective is value for money with transparency, competition, fairness and non-discrimination.
**Breakdown:**
- Over-specified or vendor-tailored specifications.
- Single-bid and limited-competition outcomes.
- Lowest-cost selection for quality-dependent services.
- Weak contract management after award.
- Inadequate capacity to evaluate technical bids.
- Integrity tools: conflict-of-interest disclosure, debarment, independent complaints before award, audit of the award decision.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 13 §13.7. No procurement threshold or portal statistic asserted.
**Links:** GOV-3050, GOV-3039, GOV-3053.
**Trap:** Tighter rules raise integrity but lengthen timelines and can deter capable suppliers; say both.
**Mains hook:** Procurement value depends on specification quality, competition and contract management, none of which a rulebook supplies.
**Back:** *"Specify, compete, contract, check: corruption is only one of four failures."*

### Flashcard GOV-3053 — Procurement and the confidentiality-versus-disclosure discipline
**Front:** What does the owner say about confidentiality of adverse information in procurement?
**Definition (learn):** Confidentiality of adverse information does not extinguish the duty to disclose the risk to the competent authority (the owner's reading of a 2026 Prelims pattern).
**Breakdown:**
- Cross-reference: owner 13 §13.7 points to the Transparency and Accountability owner (owner 08, §13.0(c)).
- The 2026 Prelims Q53 key is provisional; no answer is inferred.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 13 §13.7 (no key stated).
**Links:** GOV-3052, GOV-3050.
**Trap:** Do not treat a confidentiality clause as a bar to reporting a material risk.
**Mains hook:** Integrity in procurement requires that adverse information reaches the competent authority.
**Back:** *"Keep the bid confidential, not the risk."*

### Flashcard GOV-3054 — Gender budgeting (not owned by this file)
**Front:** What is gender budgeting, and where does it sit in the Indian budget?
**Definition (learn):** A budget practice that tags or analyses allocations for their effect on women and girls, to show how public spending serves gender equality.
**Breakdown:**
- India uses a Gender Budget Statement in the Union Budget documents (⚠️ standard knowledge; the year of introduction was not confirmed by fetch).
- The fetched Wikipedia gender-budgeting page confirms only that India is among Asian countries that have practised gender budgeting for over a decade; it gave no Indian year, ministry or document.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ Introduction year and ministry not verified; do not state from memory.
**Links:** GOV-3051, GOV-3055, and the Social Justice owner for gender entitlements (cross-owner).
**Trap:** Gender budgeting is a tagging and analysis tool, not a guarantee of allocation to women.
**Mains hook:** Gender budgeting shows whether spend is equitably targeted, not whether outcomes improved.
**Back:** *"Tag the spend, then test the effect."*

### Flashcard GOV-3055 — Green budgeting (not owned by this file)
**Front:** What is green budgeting, and what is the caution in exams?
**Definition (learn):** Analysing budget allocations for their environmental effect, including climate-related spending.
**Breakdown:**
- A general fiscal concept; this owner does not assert a specific Indian instrument or date.
- Analysis of budget tags requires an agreed methodology.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ No Indian green-budget instrument or year is verified here.
**Links:** GOV-3054, GOV-3051; Environment and Economy owners (cross-owner).
**Trap:** Do not attribute a specific green-budget statement or year to India without a source.
**Mains hook:** Use green budgeting as an analytical lens when a question asks about environmental outcomes of spending.
**Back:** *"Green tags, not green guarantees."*

### Flashcard GOV-3056 — Leakage, exclusion and inefficiency: three problems, three instruments
**Front:** Why must leakage, exclusion and inefficiency be separated?
**Definition (learn):** Three distinct welfare-delivery failures, often merged into one word.
**Breakdown:**
- Leakage: benefit diverted to ineligible or fake recipients; instrument is de-duplication.
- Exclusion: eligible person never receives it; instrument is assisted access and grievance.
- Inefficiency: benefit delivered at excessive cost; instrument is procurement and cost discipline.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 13 §13.6 (framework).
**Links:** GOV-3042, GOV-3043, GOV-3052.
**Trap:** "Plug the leakages" recommendations rarely change outcomes if exclusion or inefficiency is the real failure.
**Mains hook:** Name which of the three the evidence shows before recommending a fix.
**Back:** *"Diverted, denied, dear: three failures, three fixes."*

### Flashcard GOV-3057 — PYQ: 2022 GS-II Q8 (DBT as reform and its limitations)
**Front:** Comment on Direct Benefit Transfer as a reform and its limitations. (2022 GS-II Q8, 10 marks, 150 words.)
**Definition (learn):** A "comment" stem tests balance: what DBT reformed, its limits and its correctives.
**Breakdown:**
- Step 1: DBT changed the route of a benefit to authenticated transfer to a verified person.
- Step 2: population-scale identity plus bank access enables individual de-duplication.
- Step 3: scope: cash, in-kind, service enablers.
- Step 4: limits: leakage not targeting; exclusion; last-mile cash access; cash not a substitute for provision; weak grievance; savings versus net welfare.
- Step 5: concede the real gains; then correctives.
- Verdict: a successful payments reform asked to carry targeting and provision.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2022 GS-II Q8 (✅ owner 13 §13.0).
**Links:** GOV-3041, GOV-3042, GOV-3043, GOV-3044.
**Trap:** Do not cite a cumulative savings figure or beneficiary count.
**Mains hook:** Use the mechanism and the E1/E2 frame for a balanced comment.
**Back:** *"Route fixed, list untouched, savings counted, exclusion unmeasured."*

### Flashcard GOV-3058 — Trap pairs: JAM, DBT, PFMS, SNA, GFR
**Front:** Which of the public-finance tools are most often confused?
**Definition (learn):** Each tool solves a different problem.
**Breakdown:**
- JAM: identity and account foundation.
- DBT: beneficiary-level transfer; identity-based leakage.
- PFMS: fund-flow tracking from sanction to utilisation across schemes; agency-level diversion.
- SNA and SNA-SPARSH: Union-to-State release discipline for CSS.
- GFR: procedural rulebook.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 13 §13.4 and advanced §8.
**Links:** GOV-3040, GOV-3041, GOV-3045, GOV-3046, GOV-3047, GOV-3050.
**Trap:** "DBT, PFMS and SNA are three names for one system" is wrong.
**Mains hook:** A stage-by-stage answer shows the examiner which tool fixes which failure.
**Back:** *"Jan-Aadhaar-Mobile identifies; DBT pays; PFMS tracks; SNA releases; GFR regulates."*

### Flashcard GOV-3059 — Budget allocation, release, expenditure, utilisation and outcome
**Front:** Why is allocation not release, release not expenditure, and utilisation certificate not outcome?
**Definition (learn):** Five separate evidentiary stages in public finance.
**Breakdown:**
- Allocation: money set aside in the Budget.
- Release: money moved to the agency.
- Expenditure: money spent.
- Utilisation certificate: attestation that money was used for the sanctioned purpose.
- Outcome: change in the target population.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 13 semantic ledger (distinction list).
**Links:** GOV-3039, GOV-3051, GOV-3045.
**Trap:** A utilisation certificate proves a spending claim, not impact.
**Mains hook:** Each stage needs its own evidence before a success claim is made.
**Back:** *"Allot, release, spend, certify, achieve: five stages, five proofs."*

### Flashcard GOV-3060 — Just-in-time release and the Centre–State trade-off
**Front:** What is the fiscal-federalism cost of just-in-time release?
**Definition (learn):** Union-level efficiency and visibility come at the cost of State buffer and cash-planning flexibility.
**Breakdown:**
- Union gains real-time visibility and control.
- States lose float and face reconciliation and contribution-matching duties.
- Same tension arises in GST compensation and CSS design generally (⚠️ owner 13 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 13 §13.8 and advanced §6.
**Links:** GOV-3047, GOV-3048, GOV-3038.
**Trap:** Presenting SNA-SPARSH as a costless efficiency gain ignores State costs.
**Mains hook:** The reform is defensible only if the State-level cost is acknowledged and negotiated.
**Back:** *"The Union saves float; the State loses buffer."*

### Flashcard GOV-3061 — Aadhaar authentication: the exclusion risk of the same mechanism
**Front:** Why does the same verification that removes fake beneficiaries also cause exclusion?
**Definition (learn):** Biometric or connectivity failure can deny access to genuine beneficiaries even as it removes duplicates.
**Breakdown:**
- Vulnerable subsets: manual labourers with worn fingerprints, remote low-connectivity areas, elderly users.
- Manual override reintroduces some discretion-based leakage.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 13 advanced §5 (boundary case, no statistic asserted).
**Links:** GOV-3043, GOV-3040, GOV-3042.
**Trap:** Assuming authentication failure is rare or random; it falls on the least-equipped.
**Mains hook:** Efficiency gain and exclusion risk arise from the same verification mechanism; a rigorous answer shows both.
**Back:** *"The same fingerprint that catches the ghost can miss the grandmother."*

### Flashcard GOV-3062 — Last-mile cash access and the banking correspondent
**Front:** Why is a credited bank account not money in hand?
**Definition (learn):** Last-mile access depends on banking correspondents, connectivity and distance.
**Breakdown:**
- Banking correspondent is the actual last mile in much of rural India, with commission incentives (✅ owner 13 §13.8).
- Dormant or frozen accounts and name mismatches add to exclusion.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 13 §13.8 (no commission rate asserted).
**Links:** GOV-3040, GOV-3043, GOV-3061.
**Trap:** Account opened is not withdrawal available.
**Mains hook:** Measure exclusion, not only savings, to see the last-mile failure.
**Back:** *"Credit is not cash: the correspondent is the last mile."*

### Flashcard GOV-3063 — Cash transfer versus in-kind provision
**Front:** When is cash transfer a poorer instrument than in-kind provision?
**Definition (learn):** Cash respects choice but shifts price risk to the recipient; kind guarantees the good but needs a supply chain.
**Breakdown:**
- Cash fits where a market exists and prices are stable.
- In-kind fits where markets are absent or volatile.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 13 §13.8 (analytical).
**Links:** GOV-3041, GOV-3057.
**Trap:** Transferring cash where no market exists shifts price risk to the poorest.
**Mains hook:** Cash versus kind is a design choice; state the market condition that decides it.
**Back:** *"Cash chooses for you; kind delivers for you, if the shop exists."*

### Flashcard GOV-3064 — Rules-based GFR versus digital tracking via PFMS
**Front:** How do GFR's ex-ante rules differ from PFMS's ex-post tracking?
**Definition (learn):** GFR sets procedure before spending; PFMS monitors flows during and after.
**Breakdown:**
- GFR: sanction powers, procurement procedure, expenditure controls.
- PFMS: real-time visibility of fund flow and utilisation.
- Complementary: rules plus monitoring; neither replaces the other.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 13 advanced §2.
**Links:** GOV-3050, GOV-3045, GOV-3058.
**Trap:** Tracking is not authorisation; a monitored flow can still have been improperly sanctioned.
**Mains hook:** A complete public-finance answer needs ex-ante rules and ex-post tracking together.
**Back:** *"GFR says how to spend; PFMS says where it went."*

### Flashcard GOV-3065 — Output-Outcome Monitoring Framework link to public finance
**Front:** How does the Output-Outcome Monitoring Framework relate to public-finance tools?
**Definition (learn):** OOMF is a Union Budget requirement that covered schemes state outputs and outcomes with indicators, linking allocation to results. The M&E group owns the detail.
**Breakdown:**
- Budget-level dual reporting.
- Public-finance tools (PFMS, DBT) supply data for outcome evaluation.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 13 §12 (study link to owner 15).
**Links:** GOV-3051, GOV-3039.
**Trap:** Coverage of OOMF changes each budget year; do not carry forward counts (see M&E group).
**Mains hook:** Use fund-flow data as input to outcome judgement, not as outcome itself.
**Back:** *"Budget tells you what it promised; OOMF asks what changed."*

### GROUP — Participatory Governance

### Flashcard GOV-3066 — Participatory governance: voice, authority and quality
**Front:** What does participatory governance distribute, and what makes it real?
**Definition (learn):** Participatory governance spreads voice across information, consultation, deliberation, co-production, monitoring and sometimes delegated decision-making. Quality depends on inclusion, information, agenda control, representativeness and feedback.
**Breakdown:**
- Information, consultation, involvement, co-decision, co-production, delegated control (ladder, GOV-3073).
- Real participation changes outcomes only where the forum holds authority over the decision.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §1 and §13.4.
**Links:** GOV-3067, GOV-3068, GOV-3073, GOV-3072.
**Trap:** Attendance, quorum or comment counts do not prove representativeness or shared decision power.
**Mains hook:** Institutionalised forms of participation do not guarantee substance; test authority, inclusion and feedback.
**Back:** *"Forms are many, substance is rare: test authority first."*

### Flashcard GOV-3067 — Subsidiarity: the information and execution principle
**Front:** What is subsidiarity, and why is it an efficiency argument?
**Definition (learn):** Decisions should be taken at the lowest level capable of handling them effectively, closest to those affected and to the relevant information.
**Breakdown:**
- Efficiency rationale: decisions far from information and execution are worse, regardless of resources.
- Democratic value is a further argument, not the primary one in the 2025 PYQ framing.
- Higher levels may intervene where the lower level genuinely cannot act effectively.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §2 and advanced §2.
**Links:** GOV-3066, GOV-3076, GOV-3090, GOV-3083.
**Trap:** Subsidiarity does not mean central or State government must never intervene (✅ owner 14 trap).
**Mains hook:** Subsidiarity is an information argument before it is a democratic one.
**Back:** *"Decide where the facts are: as low as capable, not as low as possible."*

### Flashcard GOV-3068 — Participation quality versus participation frequency
**Front:** How do you distinguish a genuine participatory forum from a ceremonial one?
**Definition (learn):** Participation frequency counts how often meetings happen. Participation quality asks whether the forum influences decisions.
**Breakdown:**
- Frequency can be met while the decision was taken elsewhere.
- Quality tests: authority, timing, information, inclusion, record and response, feedback loop.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §2 and §13.6.
**Links:** GOV-3015, GOV-3089, GOV-3094.
**Trap:** A Gram Sabha held on schedule is not proof of genuine participation (✅ owner 14 trap).
**Mains hook:** India has institutionalised the forms of participation more than their substance.
**Back:** *"Count the meetings, then count the decisions they changed."*

### Flashcard GOV-3069 — Participatory budgeting
**Front:** What is participatory budgeting, and what is its Indian scale?
**Definition (learn):** A process that lets citizens directly deliberate and decide on the allocation of a defined part of the public budget. Global reference: Porto Alegre, Brazil.
**Breakdown:**
- Co-decision on a defined share of the budget.
- In India: scattered municipal or ward-level pilots, not a universal practice (✅ owner 14 §4).
- The most co-decisional instrument and the least institutionalised.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Porto Alegre as global model (✅ owner 14 §2). No list of Indian pilot cities asserted (❌ owner 14 §13.10).
**Links:** GOV-3072, GOV-3073, GOV-3067.
**Trap:** Participatory budgeting is not a widespread standard Indian municipal method (✅ owner 14 trap).
**Mains hook:** Use participatory budgeting as the co-decision benchmark, while admitting its pilot scale.
**Back:** *"Citizens hold a slice of the purse: rare in India, real where tried."*

### Flashcard GOV-3070 — Pre-Legislative Consultation Policy, 2014
**Front:** What does the 2014 Pre-Legislative Consultation Policy require, and what is its legal status?
**Definition (learn):** The Union's executive policy for public consultation on draft legislation before it is introduced. Draft bills and explanatory material are published, with ordinarily at least 30 days for comments, subject to stated exceptions.
**Breakdown:**
- Executive policy, not a statute.
- Comments are not binding.
- No general duty to publish a reasoned response (✅ owner 14 §13.4 ladder).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2014 policy; about 30 days ordinary comment period (✅ owner 14 §2, §13.10).
**Links:** GOV-3071, GOV-3072, GOV-3079.
**Trap:** Consultation does not guarantee that feedback changes the law; it is consultation, not co-decision (✅ owner 14 trap).
**Mains hook:** Consultation without a response matrix can be legitimation for a decision already taken.
**Back:** *"Thirty days to talk, not to bind: an executive policy, not a law."*

### Flashcard GOV-3071 — MyGov
**Front:** What is MyGov, and what does it do for participation?
**Definition (learn):** The Government of India's citizen-engagement and crowdsourcing platform, for policy ideas, public discussion and campaigns.
**Breakdown:**
- Crowdsourcing and consultation channel.
- Influence on final policy varies by initiative and is not institutionally binding.
- Digital access does not represent silent or digitally excluded groups.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §2 and §12; MyGov remains an official platform (✅ owner 14 verified 5 Sept 2026 via mygov.in). No user counts asserted (❌).
**Links:** GOV-3070, GOV-3088.
**Trap:** An online comment count is not representativeness (✅ owner 14 §12 verification).
**Mains hook:** Online consultation widens reach but excludes the digitally absent.
**Back:** *"MyGov asks the crowd; the crowd does not always answer for the silent."*

### Flashcard GOV-3072 — Consultation, co-decision and co-production
**Front:** How do consultation, co-decision and co-production differ in strength?
**Definition (learn):** Three distinct forms of citizen role in governance.
**Breakdown:**
- Consultation: asked for input; decision-maker not bound (pre-legislative consultation, MyGov).
- Co-decision: citizens determine an outcome (participatory budgeting; Gram Sabha approvals under PESA in Fifth Schedule areas).
- Co-production: citizens produce outcomes jointly (community monitoring, user groups, SHG delivery, social audit).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 advanced §2 and §13.4.
**Links:** GOV-3069, GOV-3070, GOV-3073, GOV-3012, GOV-3093.
**Trap:** Conflating consultation with co-decision overstates the strength of consultation mechanisms (✅ owner 14 advanced trap).
**Mains hook:** Specify which form a proposed reform actually creates.
**Back:** *"Ask, decide, produce: three rungs, three powers."*

### Flashcard GOV-3073 — The participation ladder and the paradox
**Front:** What are the rungs of the participation ladder, and what is the paradox India presents?
**Definition (learn):** The ladder orders what a citizen actually gets: information, consultation, involvement, co-decision, co-production, delegated control.
**Breakdown:**
- Information: told after the decision.
- Consultation: asked; not binding.
- Involvement: takes part in planning.
- Co-decision: determines the outcome.
- Co-production: produces the outcome jointly.
- Delegated control: runs the service.
- Paradox: the most institutionalised instruments (pre-legislative consultation, MyGov) sit on the weakest rungs; the most co-decisional (participatory budgeting) is the least institutionalised.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §13.4 (analytical ladder).
**Links:** GOV-3069, GOV-3070, GOV-3071, GOV-3072.
**Trap:** Rung placement depends on the decision power, not the number of meetings.
**Mains hook:** Place any mechanism on the ladder and ask whether it has authority over the outcome.
**Back:** *"Information, consult, involve, decide, produce, run: where is your citizen?"*

### Flashcard GOV-3074 — Ward Committees and Article 243S
**Front:** Which municipalities must have Ward Committees under Article 243S, and what does State law decide?
**Definition (learn):** Ward Committees are municipal participatory bodies. Article 243S requires them in municipalities with a population of three lakh or more; State law determines composition and details.
**Breakdown:**
- Population threshold: three lakh or more.
- Composition set by State law.
- Urban participation gap is structural (✅ owner 14 §13.8).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Art 243S; three lakh or more (✅ owner 14 §2, §13.10).
**Links:** GOV-3037, GOV-3015, GOV-3075.
**Trap:** Art 243S does not require ward committees in every municipality, only those at the population threshold (owner 14 §13.10); ⚠️ state-level functioning counts are not asserted.
**Mains hook:** Urban participation is weaker than rural because the constitutional requirement is threshold-based and the composition is State-set.
**Back:** *"Three lakh wards, state-drawn rules."*

### Flashcard GOV-3075 — Gram Sabha ratification: the trap of pre-decided plans
**Front:** When is a Gram Sabha meeting a formality rather than participation?
**Definition (learn):** When it ratifies a Panchayat plan already decided without substantive discussion.
**Breakdown:**
- Participation frequency without quality.
- Test: could the Sabha have changed the decision, and did it record objections?
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner 14 §5 illustration (analytical).
**Links:** GOV-3068, GOV-3015, GOV-3089.
**Trap:** A quorum recorded is not a decision made.
**Mains hook:** Gram Sabha effectiveness is about authority and deliberation, not attendance.
**Back:** *"Ratified is not decided: the Sabha must be able to say no."*

### Flashcard GOV-3076 — Why decisions drift upward: the causal chain
**Front:** What chain pushes decisions away from information and execution?
**Definition (learn):** Administrative convenience, capacity arguments, political-control incentives, information loss in transmission, and outcome mismatch.
**Breakdown:**
- Administrative convenience: one design is simpler than many local variants.
- Capacity argument: real but self-fulfilling if capacity is never built because authority is never given.
- Political control: discretionary allocation carries political credit.
- Information loss: local report, block, district, State, Union, filtered at every step.
- Outcome mismatch: standard design unsuited to terrain, social structure or local capacity.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §13.5 and advanced §3 (analytical).
**Links:** GOV-3067, GOV-3083, GOV-3077.
**Trap:** Capacity is not always a true constraint; sometimes it is a self-fulfilling justification.
**Mains hook:** Diagnose why centralisation persists before criticising it.
**Back:** *"Convenience, capacity, control, lost information, wrong design."*

### Flashcard GOV-3077 — Legitimate centralisation
**Front:** What are the legitimate reasons for some centralisation?
**Definition (learn):** Economies of scale, inter-regional equity, technical capacity gaps and rights that must not vary locally.
**Breakdown:**
- Economies of scale: large infrastructure, national highway corridors.
- Inter-regional equity: redistributive transfers.
- Technical capacity: epidemiology, structural engineering.
- Non-discrimination rights that cannot differ across localities.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §2 and §13.7 (analytical).
**Links:** GOV-3076, GOV-3038, GOV-3067.
**Trap:** Criticising all centralisation reads as advocacy; name the legitimate cases first.
**Mains hook:** Acknowledge these rationales, then identify where centralisation goes beyond them.
**Back:** *"Scale, equity, skill, and rights: four reasons to centralise, named first."*

### Flashcard GOV-3078 — Deliberation design: eight tests of real participation
**Front:** What are the eight tests of real deliberation in a participatory mechanism?
**Definition (learn):** A checklist for judging any novel participatory mechanism.
**Breakdown:**
- 1 Authority: is the forum deciding or advising on a decided matter?
- 2 Timing: consulted before options are foreclosed?
- 3 Information: usable budget, options and trade-offs?
- 4 Inclusion: who is absent?
- 5 Facilitation: independent or run by the implementing agency?
- 6 Record and response: objections recorded and reasons published?
- 7 Feedback loop: do participants learn what happened?
- 8 Conflict settings: representative body, agreed facts, route to binding outcome.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §13.6 (analytical framework).
**Links:** GOV-3079, GOV-3084, GOV-3088.
**Trap:** A mechanism that fails authority and inclusion is consultation theatre.
**Mains hook:** Use the eight tests to evaluate a novel mechanism such as a citizen jury or community scorecard.
**Back:** *"Authority, timing, information, inclusion, facilitation, record, feedback, conflict route."*

### Flashcard GOV-3079 — Consultation feedback matrix
**Front:** What must a consultation feedback matrix contain?
**Definition (learn):** A table that records how each stakeholder's input changed or did not change a draft.
**Breakdown:**
- Stakeholder category.
- Issue raised.
- Government response.
- Accepted or rejected, with reasons.
- Resulting change to the draft.
- Unresolved distributional effect.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ recommended design from owner 14 §10 and §13.6 (not an official format).
**Links:** GOV-3070, GOV-3078.
**Trap:** Publishing "comments received" without responses is compliance, not accountability.
**Mains hook:** A consultation without a response matrix cannot be distinguished from a formality.
**Back:** *"Who said what, what changed, why not, who lost."*

### Flashcard GOV-3080 — Partnership forms: contracting, convergence, co-production, PPP
**Front:** What are the four partnership forms in development delivery?
**Definition (learn):** Four distinct ways that state, market and citizens share delivery.
**Breakdown:**
- Contracting: the state buys delivery.
- Convergence: public agencies align at the point of delivery.
- Co-production: citizens as co-producers (community monitoring, user groups, SHGs).
- Public–private partnership: risk and finance shared under contract.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §13.0(a); PPP governance block in owner 02.
**Links:** GOV-3081, GOV-3085, GOV-3072.
**Trap:** Partnership is not the same as contracting; shared work can become nobody's work.
**Mains hook:** Partnership works where the duty-holder remains identifiable.
**Back:** *"Contract buys, converge aligns, citizens co-produce, PPP shares risk."*

### Flashcard GOV-3081 — PYQ: 2019 GS-II Q17 (cooperation among service sectors and partnership)
**Front:** Examine the need for cooperation among service sectors and partnership in the development process. (2019 GS-II Q17, 15 marks, 250 words.)
**Definition (learn):** The stem asks for co-production reasoning: outcomes depend on household behaviour as well as state supply.
**Breakdown:**
- Explain why partnership is structurally necessary: sanitation is the clearest case.
- Name the four partnership forms and keep them distinct.
- Governance conditions: clear responsibility, shared information base, a single accountable authority, a dispute and exit route.
- Critical half: partnership can diffuse accountability.
- Verdict: cooperation improves outcomes where the duty-holder is identifiable.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2019 GS-II Q17 (✅ owner 14 §13.0(a)).
**Links:** GOV-3080, GOV-3085, GOV-3078.
**Trap:** Do not invent a project result or contract value.
**Mains hook:** Answer through co-production and accountability, not generic cooperation.
**Back:** *"Supply builds the latrine; behaviour gives the health outcome."*

### Flashcard GOV-3082 — PYQ: 2022 GS-II Q17 (donor dependence and community participation), participation half
**Front:** How should a 15-mark answer treat donor dependence and community participation? (2022 GS-II Q17, 15 marks, 250 words.)
**Definition (learn):** This file supplies the participation half; the donor-distortion half belongs to the Development Processes owner (owner 03).
**Breakdown:**
- Genuine participation requires authority over a decision, not consultation on it.
- Donor cycles are short; participation needs slow institution-building.
- Elite capture qualification.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2022 GS-II Q17 (✅ owner 14 §13.0(b), joint with owner 03).
**Links:** GOV-3068, GOV-3078, GOV-3088.
**Trap:** Answering only the donor half, or only the participation half, leaves the answer incomplete.
**Mains hook:** Donor projects can crowd out the slow, authority-based participation that durable institutions need.
**Back:** *"Donors time the project; participation needs years of authority."*

### Flashcard GOV-3083 — PYQ: 2025 GS-II Q17 (decision-making distant from information and execution)
**Front:** Critically evaluate: decision-making and problem-solving responsibilities are not located close to the source of information and execution, defeating the objectives of development. (2025 GS-II Q17, 15 marks.)
**Definition (learn):** A subsidiarity question with two failures: separation from information and separation from execution.
**Breakdown:**
- Step (a): define subsidiarity as an information and execution principle.
- Step (b): why centralisation persists (convenience, capacity, political control).
- Step (c): how the mismatch defeats development (misdiagnosis, delay, weak ownership).
- Step (d): subsidiarity-consistent correctives plus acknowledged legitimate centralisation.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2025 GS-II Q17 (✅ owner 14 §9; the routing ledger places this demand in the Development Processes owner, and this owner supplies the subsidiarity half, owner 14 semantic ledger).
**Links:** GOV-3067, GOV-3076, GOV-3077.
**Trap:** Treating information and execution as one problem misses the two failures.
**Mains hook:** Separate the information failure from the execution failure, then recommend genuine co-decision, not consultation alone.
**Back:** *"Two gaps: away from the facts, away from the work."*

### Flashcard GOV-3084 — PYQ: 2026 Prelims Q52 (participatory resolution of tribal land and environmental conflict)
**Front:** Participatory resolution of tribal land, waste-management and environmental conflict (2026 Prelims GS-I Q52).
**Definition (learn):** The item tests whether participation in a conflict setting is different from participation in planning.
**Breakdown:**
- Distinguish consultation, consent and co-decision.
- Gram Sabha powers in Fifth Schedule areas under PESA: consultation before land acquisition, not universal consent.
- Conditions under which deliberation can resolve, not entrench, a dispute: recognised representative body, agreed factual basis, route to a binding outcome.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2026 Prelims Q52; provisional 2026 Set-A key held locally; no option or answer inferred (✅ owner 14 §13.0(d)).
**Links:** GOV-3012, GOV-3078, GOV-3072.
**Trap:** A statement saying PESA requires Gram Sabha consent for all land acquisition is wrong. Do not infer an answer letter.
**Mains hook:** Deliberation without a route to decision turns a dispute into a grievance.
**Back:** *"Consult in PESA areas, decide only where the route is binding."*

### Flashcard GOV-3085 — Diffused accountability in partnership
**Front:** How can partnership weaken accountability?
**Definition (learn):** When several actors share a task, the citizen has no single addressee.
**Breakdown:**
- Partnership can substitute for state obligation.
- It can privilege organised partners over unorganised beneficiaries.
- Remedy: a single accountable authority for the outcome.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §13.0(a) step 5.
**Links:** GOV-3080, GOV-3081.
**Trap:** Shared responsibility is not shared accountability.
**Mains hook:** Cooperation improves outcomes only where the duty-holder remains identifiable.
**Back:** *"Four hands, no name: who answers for the outcome?"*

### Flashcard GOV-3086 — Majoritarian deliberation versus minority rights
**Front:** Can a Gram Sabha decide against a minority within the village?
**Definition (learn):** Yes. Majoritarian deliberation can override a minority; participation is not a substitute for rights protection.
**Breakdown:**
- Rights protection is a separate guarantee.
- Participation design must include safeguards for minorities and excluded groups.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ analytical counter-argument (owner 14 §13.7).
**Links:** GOV-3026, GOV-3067, GOV-3078.
**Trap:** A democratic decision is not automatically a just one.
**Mains hook:** Pair participation with rights safeguards, not with majoritarian finality.
**Back:** *"Majority votes, rights still bind."*

### Flashcard GOV-3087 — Participation versus speed
**Front:** When must a decision not wait for participatory deliberation?
**Definition (learn):** Outbreak response and disaster relief cannot wait for full deliberation.
**Breakdown:**
- The answer is differentiated design, not uniform participation.
- Emergency decisions need fast authority with later review.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ analytical (owner 14 §13.7).
**Links:** GOV-3078, GOV-3077.
**Trap:** Demanding participation for every decision ignores urgency.
**Mains hook:** Match participation depth to the urgency and reversibility of the decision.
**Back:** *"Urgent acts act first, then account."*

### Flashcard GOV-3088 — Who is absent: inclusion in participatory forums
**Front:** Which groups are systematically absent from participatory forums?
**Definition (learn):** Women, landless households, migrants, persons with disabilities and the poorest, plus those missing from the electoral-roll basis of the forum.
**Breakdown:**
- Attendance, speaking time and proxy participation are different; measure proxy participation.
- Poorest households bear the highest relative cost of attending (wages, time).
- Scheduled-Area communities have PESA powers unavailable elsewhere.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §13.6 and §13.8.
**Links:** GOV-3078, GOV-3071, GOV-3026.
**Trap:** Counting attendance as inclusion overstates participation.
**Mains hook:** Test inclusion explicitly and provide an assisted channel for the absent.
**Back:** *"Who is missing from the room decides who is missing from the plan."*

### Flashcard GOV-3089 — Consultation as legitimation
**Front:** What is the most common real-world failure of consultation?
**Definition (learn):** A comment window that manufactures consent for a decision already taken.
**Breakdown:**
- Timing: consultation after options are foreclosed.
- Remedy: consult before options close; publish response.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ analytical (owner 14 §13.7).
**Links:** GOV-3068, GOV-3070, GOV-3079.
**Trap:** Opening a 30-day window does not by itself prove consultation was genuine.
**Mains hook:** Judge consultation by timing and response, not by the calendar.
**Back:** *"Open window, closed decision: legitimation, not consultation."*

### Flashcard GOV-3090 — Subsidiarity is appropriate level, not lowest level
**Front:** Does subsidiarity always favour the most local decision-maker?
**Definition (learn):** No. Subsidiarity is a principle of the appropriate level; higher levels act where the lower level lacks capacity.
**Breakdown:**
- Capacity and equity tests apply.
- Case against over-centralisation is strong, but not absolute.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 advanced trap and §13.9.
**Links:** GOV-3067, GOV-3077.
**Trap:** "Subsidiarity means always local" is the classic wrong answer.
**Mains hook:** A credible answer names where centralisation exceeds its legitimate basis.
**Back:** *"Appropriate, not always lowest."*

### Flashcard GOV-3091 — Decentralised does not mean inclusive
**Front:** Why does devolution not automatically produce inclusive participation?
**Definition (learn):** The forum that brings decisions closer to citizens also brings them closer to locally dominant groups.
**Breakdown:**
- Elite capture risk.
- Correctives: rotation, reservation, independent facilitation, disclosure, quorum rules, separate forums for excluded groups, social audit.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 §13.2 (T3) and advanced §8.
**Links:** GOV-3026, GOV-3088, GOV-3078.
**Trap:** Decentralisation is not an automatic benefit; capture is documented.
**Mains hook:** Participation requires design, not merely devolution.
**Back:** *"Closer to citizens, closer to the powerful too: design the safeguards."*

### Flashcard GOV-3092 — Information-separation and execution-separation
**Front:** Why should the 2025-type answer treat information and execution separately?
**Definition (learn):** The stem combines two failures. Separation from information means planners lack accurate local knowledge; separation from execution means planners lack awareness of or accountability for implementation.
**Breakdown:**
- Information failure: wrong diagnosis.
- Execution failure: wrong sequencing and unreal targets.
- Each needs a different remedy.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 advanced §3 and §13.2 (analytical).
**Links:** GOV-3083, GOV-3076.
**Trap:** Merging the two into one generic point loses marks.
**Mains hook:** Analyse each failure separately, then recommend co-decision at the right level.
**Back:** *"Two gaps: knowing and doing."*

### Flashcard GOV-3093 — Consultation versus co-decision strength
**Front:** Why is consultation a weaker form of subsidiarity than co-decision?
**Definition (learn):** Consultation informs a decision the decision-maker still takes. Co-decision lets citizens determine the outcome.
**Breakdown:**
- Pre-legislative consultation: consultation-based.
- Participatory budgeting: co-decision.
- A rigorous answer names which form is at issue.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 14 advanced §2 and §7.
**Links:** GOV-3072, GOV-3070, GOV-3069.
**Trap:** Consultation mechanisms do not equal co-decision in citizen control.
**Mains hook:** Specify the form of participation before judging its strength.
**Back:** *"Consult informs, co-decide binds."*

### Flashcard GOV-3094 — Participation costs and selection bias
**Front:** Why does the cost of participation shape who participates?
**Definition (learn):** Attending meetings costs wages and time, so the poorest bear the highest relative cost, creating selection bias in who participates.
**Breakdown:**
- Selection bias: those who attend differ from those who do not.
- Remedies: timing, childcare, transport, paid time or assisted channels.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ analytical (owner 14 §13.7).
**Links:** GOV-3088, GOV-3068.
**Trap:** A high turnout can still be unrepresentative.
**Mains hook:** Judge the sample of participants, not just the count.
**Back:** *"Who can afford the meeting, decides for the rest."*

### GROUP — Monitoring, Evaluation and Outcomes

### Flashcard GOV-3095 — Output versus outcome: the M&E core
**Front:** What is the core M&E proposition, and why do outputs alone mislead?
**Definition (learn):** Counting outputs (what was built or delivered) is not the same as measuring outcomes (what changed for citizens). India's M&E architecture is designed to close that gap.
**Breakdown:**
- Output: a road built, a school constructed, a training conducted.
- Outcome: reduced travel time, improved learning, higher employability.
- Architecture: OOMF (budget-level dual reporting), DMEO (evaluation), ADP dashboard (near-real-time KPIs).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §1 and §4.
**Links:** GOV-3096, GOV-3097, GOV-3098, GOV-3100, GOV-3102.
**Trap:** Full delivery of outputs does not prove outcome achievement.
**Mains hook:** Name the rung of every statistic cited before judging a scheme.
**Back:** *"Built is output; better lives is outcome."*

### Flashcard GOV-3096 — Output and outcome: precise definitions
**Front:** Define output and outcome, and give an example of each.
**Definition (learn):** Output is what an activity directly produces. Outcome is the actual change in the target population's condition the activity was meant to bring about.
**Breakdown:**
- Output example: 90 per cent of households covered (a coverage statistic).
- Outcome example: water quality at the point of use.
- Outputs are necessary but not sufficient evidence of outcome.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §2 and §13.4 (example classification).
**Links:** GOV-3095, GOV-3097.
**Trap:** A coverage figure is an output even when it sounds like a result.
**Mains hook:** The move that earns marks: name which rung the examiner's own statistic sits on.
**Back:** *"Coverage counts the taps; quality counts the water."*

### Flashcard GOV-3097 — The results chain: input, activity, output, outcome, impact
**Front:** What are the five rungs of the results chain, and where does attribution become hardest?
**Definition (learn):** The spine of every evaluative answer.
**Breakdown:**
- Input: money, staff, materials committed.
- Activity: what was done (training conducted, pipes laid).
- Output: what was directly produced (km of road, schools built). Most Indian scheme reporting stops here.
- Outcome: change in the target population (travel time, learning).
- Impact: longer-term, wider change (market access, income); attribution is hardest here.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §13.4 (analytical ladder).
**Links:** GOV-3096, GOV-3109, GOV-3095.
**Trap:** Impact is not the same as outcome; impact is the wider, longer change and is most contested.
**Mains hook:** Trace the chain, state where the evidence stops and why.
**Back:** *"In, do, make, change, last."*

### Flashcard GOV-3098 — DMEO: formed in 2015 by merging two bodies
**Front:** What is DMEO, and which two bodies merged to form it?
**Definition (learn):** The Development Monitoring and Evaluation Office, NITI Aayog's office for monitoring and evaluating government programmes.
**Breakdown:**
- Formed in 2015 by merging the Programme Evaluation Organisation (PEO) and the Independent Evaluation Office (IEO).
- Commissions third-party evaluations; produces evidence without authority to redesign schemes.
- Constituted September 2015 (✅ owner 15 verification rechecked 5 October 2026, dmeo.gov.in).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2015; PEO and IEO; NITI Aayog (✅ owner 15 §2, verified by owner 5 Oct 2026 per the owner's semantic ledger).
**Links:** GOV-3099, GOV-3108, GOV-3095.
**Trap:** DMEO is not a body created from scratch; it consolidated two earlier bodies (✅ owner 15 trap).
**Mains hook:** Consolidation of evaluation capacity was a structural reform, but evaluation findings need line-ministry adoption.
**Back:** *"Fifteen: PEO and IEO merged into DMEO."*

### Flashcard GOV-3099 — DMEO's criteria: REESI+C+E
**Front:** What are the evaluation criteria DMEO uses, expressed as REESI+C+E?
**Definition (learn):** A ready criteria set for any "evaluate" stem.
**Breakdown:**
- R: Relevance.
- E: Effectiveness.
- E: Efficiency.
- S: Sustainability.
- I: Impact.
- C: Coherence.
- E: Equity.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §13.7 and §10 (criteria used in the owner's Mains framework).
**Links:** GOV-3098, GOV-3107, GOV-3112.
**Trap:** Criteria are a frame, not evidence; listing them does not evaluate a scheme.
**Mains hook:** Use all seven criteria in a scheme-evaluation answer, and show equity disaggregation explicitly.
**Back:** *"Relevance, Effectiveness, Efficiency, Sustainability, Impact, Coherence, Equity."*

### Flashcard GOV-3100 — Output-Outcome Monitoring Framework (OOMF)
**Front:** What is the OOMF, who administers it, and what does it require?
**Definition (learn):** An annual Union Budget document covering major Central Sector and Centrally Sponsored Schemes. It states outlay, outputs, outcomes, indicators and targets.
**Breakdown:**
- Administered by the Ministry of Finance, Budget Division, through the Union Budget process.
- Forces dual reporting: outputs and outcomes with indicators.
- Coverage changes each Budget year.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** OOMF 2026-27 covers 172 major schemes with outlay of ₹500 crore or more (📰 owner 15 §8, checked 5 Oct 2026).
**Links:** GOV-3101, GOV-3051, GOV-3065.
**Trap:** OOMF does not cover every scheme in every year.
**Mains hook:** OOMF moves budget scrutiny beyond expenditure, but outcome achievement carries no consequence in the budget.
**Back:** *"The Budget now promises outcomes, not just outlays."*

### Flashcard GOV-3101 — OOMF coverage: never carry a count forward
**Front:** Why must the 172-scheme OOMF figure be cited with its year?
**Definition (learn):** OOMF coverage is re-set each Budget cycle, so a count is valid only for the document year.
**Breakdown:**
- 2026-27 document: 172 major schemes at ₹500 crore or more.
- Earlier or later years have different counts.
- Owner 15 instructs: never carry a scheme-count across Budget years.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2026-27: 172 (📰 owner 15 §8, §13.10).
**Links:** GOV-3100.
**Trap:** A figure from one Budget year is wrong for another; the most likely factual error on this topic.
**Mains hook:** Date-stamp any scheme-count claim.
**Back:** *"Year-stamp the count, or it is wrong."*

### Flashcard GOV-3102 — Aspirational Districts Programme: scale and KPIs
**Front:** What are the ADP's district count, KPI count and themes, and what is their source date?
**Definition (learn):** NITI Aayog's programme for lagging districts, tracked through a Champions of Change dashboard and delta ranking.
**Breakdown:**
- NITI live webpage accessed 5 October 2026: 117 selected districts.
- Annual Report 2025-26: 112 operational or reporting districts.
- 49 KPIs across five themes: health and nutrition, education, agriculture and water resources, financial inclusion and skill development, and basic infrastructure.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 117 (live webpage, 5 Oct 2026); 112 (Annual Report 2025-26); 49 KPIs; five themes (✅ owner 15 §2, §13.7).
**Links:** GOV-3103, GOV-3104, GOV-3105, GOV-3112.
**Trap:** Quote either denominator only with its source and date; do not blend them.
**Mains hook:** The ADP is an outcome-adjacent dashboard, not an impact evaluation.
**Back:** *"One hundred seventeen selected, one hundred twelve reporting, forty-nine KPIs."*

### Flashcard GOV-3103 — ADP denominator trap: 117 versus 112
**Front:** Why are the ADP's 117 and 112 figures both correct, and how should they be cited?
**Definition (learn):** Two official sources with different denominators: a live webpage listing selected districts and an annual report listing operational or reporting districts.
**Breakdown:**
- Do not invent a blended count.
- Do not invent a causal explanation for the difference (owner 15 §13.10).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 117 (live page 5 Oct 2026); 112 (AR 2025-26).
**Links:** GOV-3102.
**Trap:** "The ADP covers 117 districts" without a source date and denominator is incomplete.
**Mains hook:** Cite source, date and denominator in every district-count statement.
**Back:** *"Source and date first; denominator second; never average."*

### Flashcard GOV-3104 — Champions of Change dashboard and monthly self-reporting
**Front:** How does the ADP's data pipeline work?
**Definition (learn):** District-level officers report KPI data monthly; NITI-selected agencies validate it; a public dashboard supports near-real-time comparison.
**Breakdown:**
- Two-stage design: self-report for speed, validation for reliability.
- Validation capacity varies by sector and indicator.
- Gaming pressure falls on the officer who reports the number (owner 15 §13.9).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §3 and advanced §4.
**Links:** GOV-3102, GOV-3105, GOV-3112.
**Trap:** The ADP is not free of self-reporting; the validation layer is the safeguard.
**Mains hook:** Self-report plus validation balances speed against reliability.
**Back:** *"Officers report monthly, agencies check, the dashboard shows."*

### Flashcard GOV-3105 — Delta ranking versus absolute ranking
**Front:** How does delta ranking differ from absolute ranking, and what is its risk?
**Definition (learn):** Absolute ranking compares current KPI levels. Delta ranking compares the rate of improvement over a period.
**Breakdown:**
- Absolute ranking favours already-advantaged units.
- Delta ranking rewards effort and progress regardless of starting point.
- Risk: baseline understatement can inflate apparent improvement.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 advanced §2 and §3 (analytical).
**Links:** GOV-3104, GOV-3106, GOV-3112.
**Trap:** Believing the ADP ranks districts only on absolute level is wrong; its delta system rewards pace of improvement (✅ owner 15 trap).
**Mains hook:** Delta ranking is an incentive-design choice that avoids penalising historical disadvantage, and it needs robust baselines.
**Back:** *"Absolute favours the head start; delta rewards the sprint."*

### Flashcard GOV-3106 — Aspirational Blocks Programme: a separate unit
**Front:** What is the Aspirational Blocks Programme, and why must it not be merged with the district figures?
**Definition (learn):** A parallel NITI programme at sub-district (block) level covering 513 blocks in current NITI material.
**Breakdown:**
- Block unit, block KPIs.
- Keep district and block counts and indicator sets separate.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 513 blocks (✅ owner 15 §2, §8).
**Links:** GOV-3102, GOV-3103.
**Trap:** Do not add 513 to the district count, or mix block and district KPIs.
**Mains hook:** The block programme addresses sub-district divergence the district unit masks.
**Back:** *"Blocks are five-hundred-odd, districts are a different count."*

### Flashcard GOV-3107 — Monitoring versus evaluation
**Front:** How do monitoring and evaluation differ as governance-information functions?
**Definition (learn):** Monitoring is continuous, routine and typically self-reported. Evaluation is periodic, deeper and often independent, assessing whether outcomes were achieved.
**Breakdown:**
- Monitoring: course correction, high frequency, data-quality risk.
- Evaluation: scheme redesign, credible outcome claims, slower.
- Neither substitutes for the other.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 advanced §2 and §3.
**Links:** GOV-3102, GOV-3108, GOV-3109.
**Trap:** Monitoring and evaluation are not interchangeable terms (✅ owner 15 advanced trap).
**Mains hook:** A complete M&E architecture uses both for different purposes.
**Back:** *"Monitoring watches the road; evaluation asks where it leads."*

### Flashcard GOV-3108 — Third-party evaluation and its independence
**Front:** Why does third-party evaluation matter, and what limits its independence?
**Definition (learn):** Evaluation by an entity independent of the implementing ministry or agency, reducing the conflict of interest in self-assessment.
**Breakdown:**
- DMEO commissions and coordinates third-party evaluation.
- Independence depends on who commissions, funds and can suppress the report.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ owner 15 §2 and §13.7.
**Links:** GOV-3098, GOV-3107, GOV-3112.
**Trap:** A third-party label does not guarantee independence; check commissioning and publication.
**Mains hook:** Recommend third-party evaluation where self-reported data is questioned.
**Back:** *"Independent of the owner, not just outside the building."*

### Flashcard GOV-3109 — Attribution methods and their limits
**Front:** Which evaluation methods can establish attribution, and what does each fail to show?
**Definition (learn):** The methods table in the M&E owner.
**Breakdown:**
- Before-after: the indicator moved; cannot isolate the intervention.
- With-without comparison: a plausible counterfactual; groups may differ.
- Randomised controlled trial: causal effect for the tested variant; limited external validity; ethics of randomising entitlements; misses general equilibrium.
- Quasi-experimental: causal claims that rest on stated assumptions.
- Process evaluation: why it happened; no effect size.
- Mixed methods: effect plus mechanism plus experience.
- Administrative data: low-cost tracking; quality depends on who enters it.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §13.5 (method table).
**Links:** GOV-3097, GOV-3110, GOV-3118.
**Trap:** A before-after change is not proof of impact.
**Mains hook:** Name the method, what it establishes and its limit in a 20-mark answer.
**Back:** *"Before-after moves, with-without counts, RCT proves the variant, process explains."*

### Flashcard GOV-3110 — The baseline requirement
**Front:** Why does a defensible outcome claim need a baseline?
**Definition (learn):** A baseline is an indicator value measured before the intervention. Without it no method produces a defensible outcome claim.
**Breakdown:**
- Absence of baselines is a recurring weakness in Indian scheme design (owner 15 §13.5).
- Pairs with a comparison group for attribution.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §13.5 (analytical).
**Links:** GOV-3109, GOV-3111.
**Trap:** Post-intervention data alone cannot prove change.
**Mains hook:** Name the missing baseline in any evaluation critique.
**Back:** *"No before, no proof."*

### Flashcard GOV-3111 — Goodhart's problem and indicator gaming
**Front:** What is Goodhart's problem, and what is the correction?
**Definition (learn):** When a measure becomes a target, it ceases to be a good measure. An indicator that carries consequence stops measuring and starts being produced.
**Breakdown:**
- Correction: a basket of indicators; rotation; independent audit of a sample.
- Related: output substitution, where effort shifts to what is counted.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §13.6 (analytical framework).
**Links:** GOV-3104, GOV-3113, GOV-3112.
**Trap:** A rising indicator is not evidence of rising outcome if the indicator is gamed.
**Mains hook:** Design monitoring against gaming, not only for accuracy.
**Back:** *"The target is hit; the measure is lost."*

### Flashcard GOV-3112 — Self-reported data and the validation layer
**Front:** Why is self-reported dashboard data a risk, and how is it managed?
**Definition (learn):** Entities judged by a number often generate it. Correction: third-party validation and independent sample surveys.
**Breakdown:**
- Speed versus rigour trade-off.
- A two-stage design (report then validate) is the ADP's safeguard.
- Boundary case: an implausibly large one-month jump should trigger validation.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §13.6 and advanced §5.
**Links:** GOV-3104, GOV-3111, GOV-3108.
**Trap:** The ADP is not unvalidated; the trap is assuming validation is perfect.
**Mains hook:** Pair real-time monitoring with independent validation.
**Back:** *"Self-report is fast; validation keeps it honest."*

### Flashcard GOV-3113 — Aggregation masking and output substitution
**Front:** How do averages and substitution mislead monitoring?
**Definition (learn):** Aggregation masking hides block and group-level failure inside a district average. Output substitution shifts effort from what is not counted to what is counted.
**Breakdown:**
- Correction for masking: mandatory disaggregation by gender, social group, disability and geography.
- Correction for substitution: quality and outcome indicators alongside coverage.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §13.6.
**Links:** GOV-3111, GOV-3114, GOV-3102.
**Trap:** A district average can look healthy while excluded groups fail.
**Mains hook:** Disaggregate by gender, social group, disability and geography.
**Back:** *"Averages hide the left-behind; substitution hides the unmeasured."*

### Flashcard GOV-3114 — Survivor and selection effects; definition drift
**Front:** What two measurement errors arise from who is measured and how indicators change?
**Definition (learn):** Survivor or selection effects measure only reachable beneficiaries. Definition drift changes an indicator's meaning between years.
**Breakdown:**
- Survivor effect: drop-off and exclusion must be measured explicitly (see owner 13 E2).
- Definition drift: publish definitions with the series and note breaks.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §13.6.
**Links:** GOV-3113, GOV-3043.
**Trap:** A series with changed definitions cannot be compared without a note.
**Mains hook:** Measure the excluded, and log every definition change.
**Back:** *"Count the dropped, and note the new rule."*

### Flashcard GOV-3115 — Learning versus accountability in evaluation
**Front:** Why does evaluation used for punishment undermine learning?
**Definition (learn):** When evaluation is punitive, reporting becomes defensive and honest failure data is suppressed.
**Breakdown:**
- Separate learning uses from accountability uses; protect the first.
- The dominant Indian use is compliance, which is why findings accumulate without changing design (owner 15 T4).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §13.2 (T4) and §13.8 (analytical).
**Links:** GOV-3107, GOV-3095.
**Trap:** Publishing findings is itself accountability; an unpublished evaluation cannot be contested.
**Mains hook:** Institutional design must separate learning from punishment.
**Back:** *"Punish the report, lose the truth."*

### Flashcard GOV-3116 — Measurement versus measurability bias
**Front:** What is measurement bias, and how does it distort priorities?
**Definition (learn):** What is easy to count crowds out what matters.
**Breakdown:**
- Examples: enrolment over learning, toilets built over toilets used, camps held over conditions treated.
- Remedy: count the matter, not only the countable.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ analytical (owner 15 §13.8).
**Links:** GOV-3097, GOV-3111.
**Trap:** A high count of an easy indicator says nothing about the hard one.
**Mains hook:** Name the countable proxy and the outcome it stands for.
**Back:** *"Easy counts crowd out the hard truths."*

### Flashcard GOV-3117 — Evaluation cost and proportionality
**Front:** When is rigorous evaluation justified, and when should monitoring suffice?
**Definition (learn):** Rigorous evaluation is costly and slow. Evaluate the large and the novel; monitor the routine.
**Breakdown:**
- Proportionality is the test.
- Small programmes may not justify full evaluation.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ⚠️ analytical (owner 15 §13.8).
**Links:** GOV-3107, GOV-3108.
**Trap:** Evaluating everything is as poor design as evaluating nothing.
**Mains hook:** Argue for proportionate evaluation design.
**Back:** *"Evaluate the big and the new; watch the routine."*

### Flashcard GOV-3118 — Outcome budgeting: maturity and causal attribution
**Front:** Why is full outcome budgeting still a work in progress?
**Definition (learn):** Full outcome budgeting needs credible causal attribution. Most Indian evaluations approximate it through before-after comparison.
**Breakdown:**
- OOMF formal requirement exists, but causal attribution is methodologically hard.
- Outcome targets can be set undemandingly.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 advanced §3 and §6 (analytical).
**Links:** GOV-3051, GOV-3109.
**Trap:** Formal requirement is not the same as demonstrated maturity (✅ owner 15 advanced trap).
**Mains hook:** Say the reform is formal and partial, and name the attribution gap.
**Back:** *"Required on paper, proven in few schemes."*

### Flashcard GOV-3119 — Dashboard is not impact evaluation
**Front:** Can a dashboard, rank or correlation prove that a scheme caused an outcome?
**Definition (learn):** No. Causal claims need baseline, counterfactual, denominator and attribution controls.
**Breakdown:**
- Dashboard: monitors.
- Rank: compares.
- Correlation: co-movement, not cause.
- Impact evaluation: causal design.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 semantic ledger (verified 5 Oct 2026).
**Links:** GOV-3109, GOV-3110, GOV-3102.
**Trap:** A district climbing an ADP rank does not prove the programme caused the change.
**Mains hook:** Separate the monitoring signal from a causal claim in every answer.
**Back:** *"A rank is a rank, not a cause."*

### Flashcard GOV-3120 — Officer gaming pressure
**Front:** Why is the district officer the strongest source of gaming pressure?
**Definition (learn):** The officer who reports the number is judged by it.
**Breakdown:**
- Generates the data and is ranked on it.
- Correction: independent validation and sample audit.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §13.9.
**Links:** GOV-3104, GOV-3111, GOV-3112.
**Trap:** Blaming the officer alone misses the incentive design that creates the pressure.
**Mains hook:** Fix the incentive, not only the officer.
**Back:** *"Judged by the number, reporting the number."*

### Flashcard GOV-3121 — Usable indicator criteria
**Front:** What makes a usable indicator?
**Definition (learn):** Specific, measurable at a defined frequency, attributable to the intervention, independently verifiable, disaggregable by group and geography, and resistant to manipulation.
**Breakdown:**
- Specific and measurable.
- Frequency defined.
- Attributable.
- Verifiable.
- Disaggregable.
- Manipulation-resistant.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 §13.6.
**Links:** GOV-3111, GOV-3113, GOV-3114.
**Trap:** An indicator that fails attribution cannot prove outcome.
**Mains hook:** Test any proposed indicator against all six criteria.
**Back:** *"Specific, timed, attributable, checkable, split, and hard to fake."*

### Flashcard GOV-3122 — M&E PYQ coverage: no direct routed demand
**Front:** Has the M&E topic been asked directly in the audited Mains or Prelims routing?
**Definition (learn):** No direct General Studies Mains demand and no applied objective demand in the 2018-2023, 2024-2025 or 2026 routing ledgers is routed to this owner.
**Breakdown:**
- The topic appears as the evaluative half of demands owned elsewhere, such as scheme effectiveness, governance quality or expenditure scrutiny.
- No question is invented; the owner's answers are built for transfer.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 15 semantic ledger.
**Links:** GOV-3095, GOV-3107; cross-owner evaluation examples: GOV-3057 (DBT) and GOV-3031 (decentralisation).
**Trap:** Do not assume a remembered PYQ exists for M&E.
**Mains hook:** Use output-outcome and REESI+C+E as the transferable frame for any unfamiliar evaluation stem.
**Back:** *"No routed question, a transferable frame."*

### GROUP — Sports Governance, Institutions and Major Tournaments

### Flashcard GOV-3123 — Sports governance: autonomy versus accountability
**Front:** What is the central tension in sports governance?
**Definition (learn):** Sports bodies need technical autonomy to frame competition rules, yet monopoly control over recognition, selection and entry creates risks of opacity, conflict of interest and weak remedies.
**Breakdown:**
- Autonomy protects sporting and technical decisions.
- Accountability covers money, selection, safeguarding, elections and rights.
- Autonomy is not immunity from national law, anti-doping, financial accountability or judicial review.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 16 §5 and advanced §1.
**Links:** GOV-3124, GOV-3145, GOV-3146.
**Trap:** Autonomy never exempts a body from law or rights duties.
**Mains hook:** Autonomy is a shield for sporting decisions, not a shield against accountability.
**Back:** *"Autonomy for the game, accountability for the money and the athletes."*

### Flashcard GOV-3124 — The governance map: layers and parallel tracks
**Front:** What are the layers of sports governance, and what runs in parallel?
**Definition (learn):** A chain of rule-making and organisation, with two parallel tracks for integrity and disputes.
**Breakdown:**
- IOC governs the Olympic Movement and Games.
- International federation: common sport rules and recognition.
- National federation: governs the sport nationally, subject to recognition and law.
- Tournament organiser or league: event entry and selection.
- Athlete: eligibility, entry, discipline and safeguarding.
- Parallel: WADA Code to national anti-doping body. Dispute: internal appeal to sport tribunal or CAS where applicable.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 16 §1.
**Links:** GOV-3125, GOV-3126, GOV-3127, GOV-3128.
**Trap:** Each layer has a distinct function; do not merge them.
**Mains hook:** Trace the athlete's rule-making chain and its appeal route.
**Back:** *"Olympics, federation, national body, organiser, athlete; WADA and CAS run beside them."*

### Flashcard GOV-3125 — Institution types compared
**Front:** What is the primary role of each sports institution?
**Definition (learn):** A comparison table.
**Breakdown:**
- IOC: Olympic Movement and Olympic Games governance.
- International federation: sport-specific international rules.
- National sports federation: national representation, subject to recognition.
- WADA: global anti-doping code and standards; no tournaments.
- NADA India: national anti-doping organisation.
- CAS: independent sports-dispute forum.
- Ministry of Youth Affairs and Sports: Union policy and recognition framework.
- Sports Authority of India: training, infrastructure and athlete support.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 16 §2.
**Links:** GOV-3126, GOV-3127, GOV-3128, GOV-3135.
**Trap:** The Ministry is executive policy; SAI is an autonomous implementing body under it.
**Mains hook:** Map each institution to its function before diagnosing a failure.
**Back:** *"IOC games, federation rules, WADA code, NADA tests, CAS rules disputes, ministry sets policy."*

### Flashcard GOV-3126 — IOC versus international federations
**Front:** Does the IOC make the ordinary rules of every sport?
**Definition (learn):** No. International federations govern sport-specific rules; the IOC governs the Olympic Movement and the Games.
**Breakdown:**
- IOC: Olympic Movement and Games.
- International federation: rules of its own sport.
- Organiser: entry conditions of its own event.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 16 §2 and §5.
**Links:** GOV-3124, GOV-3125.
**Trap:** "IOC governs every sport's rules" is a classic wrong option (❌ owner 16 §5).
**Mains hook:** Layered rule-making is the reason federations need recognition and accountability in their own right.
**Back:** *"IOC runs the Games; each federation runs its sport."*

### Flashcard GOV-3127 — WADA: code writer, not tournament organiser
**Front:** What does WADA do, and what does it not do?
**Definition (learn):** WADA frames the global World Anti-Doping Code and international standards. National anti-doping organisations implement testing and results management.
**Breakdown:**
- Writes the Code and standards.
- Does not organise competitions.
- National bodies test and manage results within jurisdiction.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 16 §2 and §6.2.
**Links:** GOV-3133, GOV-3138, GOV-3125.
**Trap:** WADA is not the national anti-doping agency; NADA India is the national body.
**Mains hook:** Anti-doping is layered: WADA Code, national statute, results management, CAS appeal.
**Back:** *"WADA writes the code; the nation catches the cheats."*

### Flashcard GOV-3128 — Court of Arbitration for Sport (CAS)
**Front:** What is CAS, where is it based, and when was it established?
**Definition (learn):** An international arbitral institution based in Lausanne and established in 1984.
**Breakdown:**
- Arbitral forum, not a court of any state.
- Not a rule-maker for any sport.
- Adjudication differs from tournament administration.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Lausanne; 1984 (✅ owner 16 §6.3 and §7.8).
**Links:** GOV-3124, GOV-3127, GOV-3138.
**Trap:** CAS does not write sport rules and is not a national court.
**Mains hook:** CAS gives an independent appeal route for doping and selection disputes, without national court jurisdiction.
**Back:** *"Lausanne, 1984: an arbitrator, not a legislator."*

### Flashcard GOV-3129 — National Sports Governance Act, 2025: the timeline
**Front:** What is the statutory timeline of the National Sports Governance Act, 2025?
**Definition (learn):** India's first national statute for sports governance, passed in 2025.
**Breakdown:**
- Introduced in Lok Sabha: 23 July 2025.
- Passed: Lok Sabha 11 August 2025; Rajya Sabha 12 August 2025.
- Assent: 18 August 2025, as Act No. 25 of 2025.
- Rules framed during 2026; contents to be verified before citation.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** ✅ owner 16 §6.1 (verified by owner on 13 August 2026).
**Links:** GOV-3130, GOV-3131, GOV-3132.
**Trap:** Enacted is not the same as commenced; enacted is not the same as operational.
**Mains hook:** Name the Act and its commencement stage, not merely its existence.
**Back:** *"Twenty-three July introduced, twelve August passed, eighteenth assented: Act twenty-five."*

### Flashcard GOV-3130 — National Sports Governance Act, 2025: commencement tranches
**Front:** Which tranches of the Act have commenced, and what do they include?
**Definition (learn):** Phased commencement.
**Breakdown:**
- Select provisions from 1 January 2026.
- Second tranche from 12 May 2026 under S.O. 2406(E), commencing ss.5(3)–(5), s.7, s.17(8)–(9), ss.18, 19, 21, 28 and 29 (✅ Gazette-verified, owner 16 §7.8).
- The principal recognition-power provision was not located in a commenced tranche as of 13 August 2026.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1 Jan 2026; 12 May 2026; S.O. 2406(E) (✅ owner 16 §6.1, §7.8).
**Links:** GOV-3129, GOV-3131, GOV-3132.
**Trap:** Do not claim the Board is already exercising recognition powers.
**Mains hook:** Governance transition is incomplete; say so.
**Back:** *"January selects, May completes the first phase; recognition still waits."*

### Flashcard GOV-3131 — National Sports Board and National Sports Tribunal
**Front:** What are the National Sports Board and National Sports Tribunal under the 2025 Act?
**Definition (learn):** Statutory bodies created in law by the Act.
**Breakdown:**
- National Sports Board: recognition and regulatory jurisdiction designed by the Act; recognition power not located in a commenced tranche by 13 August 2026.
- National Sports Tribunal: statutory forum for sports disputes; jurisdiction and appeal provisions not located in commenced tranches by the same date.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As of 13 August 2026 (✅ owner 16 §6.1).
**Links:** GOV-3130, GOV-3132, GOV-3135.
**Trap:** Do not assert that the Board has derecognised a federation or the Tribunal has decided any case (❌ owner 16 §6.1).
**Mains hook:** Creation in law is not operational jurisdiction.
**Back:** *"Created in law, not yet in practice."*

### Flashcard GOV-3132 — National Sports Development Code of India, 2011: the operative framework
**Front:** Which instrument governs recognition of national federations during India's transition?
**Definition (learn):** The National Sports Development Code of India, 2011, read with the Act's deeming continuity for previously recognised bodies, remains the operative recognition framework until the Act's recognition jurisdiction is commenced and operationalised.
**Breakdown:**
- Administrative instrument, not a statute.
- Deeming protection for previously recognised bodies.
- Transition to the Act is incomplete as of 13 August 2026.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Code of India, 2011 (✅ owner 16 §6.1 and §6.3).
**Links:** GOV-3129, GOV-3131, GOV-3125.
**Trap:** The Act did not immediately replace the 2011 Code; do not describe an uncommenced regime as in force.
**Mains hook:** Recognition continues under a 2011 administrative code; the statutory regime is still arriving.
**Back:** *"Twenty-eleven code holds the fort until the Act commences."*

### Flashcard GOV-3133 — National Anti-Doping Act, 2022 and NADA's status
**Front:** What did the National Anti-Doping Act, 2022 change for NADA?
